# QAL Phase 3a — Service Runtime (HTTP, `net`, `db`, Security Carry-overs) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** A stored QAL handler `fn(HttpRequest) -> HttpResponse` runs as an HTTP/1.1 service (`qal serve`) and through the same pipeline without TCP (`qal request`, MCP `qal_request`), with the effects `net` (outgoing HTTP with host allowlist and SSRF protection) and `db` (SQLite through the host, literal-only parameterized SQL, one transaction per execution), plus the security carry-overs (`fs` through verified directory descriptors, host buffers counted against the memory limit, a write budget for `fs`), store schema 3 with package export/import, and an empty, valid `qal/std/std.qalpkg`.

**Architecture:** `qal-core` gains the prelude types (`HttpRequest`, `SqlValue`, ... — referenced by name in canonical forms, never by hash) and a static SQL checker; `qal-check` types the built-ins `http`, `db_exec`, `db_query` and enforces SQL literals (E0241/E0242); `qal-wasm` imports `net.http`, `db.exec`, `db.query`, whose host side builds the whole `Result` value in guest memory. `qal-run` gets a general encoder for records and enums, an exact decoder, the `db`/`net`/`fs` hosts, budgets, and a *prepared* runner (module linked once, a fresh store per execution from wasmtime's pooling allocator, deadlines from an epoch ticker). The new crate `qal-serve` has the request pipeline (`Service::handle`), the HTTP layer (`httparse`) and the synchronous server (accept thread, bounded queue, worker threads, JSON log, graceful shutdown). `qal-cli` adds `qal serve`, `qal request`, `qal export`, `qal import` and the MCP tool `qal_request`; `qal-store` gets schema 3 (`meta`) and packages.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 1.97 (edition 2021); existing `wasmtime =49.0.1` (now with feature `pooling-allocator`), `rusqlite =0.40.2` (`qal-run` adds feature `hooks` for the authorizer), `serde`/`serde_json` 1, `sha2 =0.11.0`, `wasm-encoder`/`wasmparser =0.259.0`. **New, pinned exactly** (all published at least two weeks before 2026-10-15, dates from the crates.io API): `httparse =1.10.1` (2025-03-03), `signal-hook =0.4.4` (2026-04-04; not 0.4.5 of 2026-10-04), `rustix =1.1.5` (2026-09-16, already in the lock through wasmtime), `ureq =2.12.1` (2024-12-04, `default-features = false, features = ["tls"]`: rustls with web-PKI roots, no gzip, no proxy support), `url =2.5.8` (2026-01-05). Every package new to `qal/Cargo.lock` was checked the same way; two too-new transitive versions are pinned lower in the lock (`rustls 0.23.45` instead of 0.23.46 of 2026-10-14, `zeroize 1.9.0` instead of 1.9.1 of 2026-10-06). The lock changes are given as edits in the tasks that add the dependencies.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-qal-phase3-design.md` (binding; §2 service model, §3 `net`/`db`, §4.3 packages, §5 security, §6 codes, §7 CLI/MCP, §9 phase 3a). Roadmap: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-qal-roadmap.md` (row 3, carry-over "Phase 3"). Previous plans: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-qal-phase1d-mcp-server.md`, `...-phase1e-benchmark.md`. Phase 3a does not depend on Phase 2 (spec §9).

**Sub-plan context:** Phase 3 is three plans: **3a runtime and service layer (this plan)**, 3b standard library in QAL written by agents (needs 3a and Phase-2 generics), 3c todo backend and acceptance (needs 3b).

**How this plan was made:** every file below was first implemented test-first in a scratch copy of the repository and verified after each task (318 tests at the end in 21 test binaries, every one `ok`, zero warnings, the no-panic clippy command clean, the performance gate measured). An independent reviewer then attacked it (SSRF, SQL, HTTP parsing, isolation, timeouts, `openat`, budgets, panics); the confirmed findings are fixed and pinned by tests (Task 9). The plan was generated by a script that replayed every step on a fresh export of the repository HEAD, ran each command and recorded its real outcome, and checked after every task that the tree is byte-identical to the verified state — the code blocks are exactly that code.

## Global Constraints

- Workspace `qland/qal/`, branch `Mac`. Stage only the paths named in each commit step (never `git add -A`; the user's uncommitted `qlang/web/index.html` and the untracked `bench/` files stay untouched). Do not push.
- Dependencies exactly as pinned in the Tech Stack line; `qal/Cargo.lock` is tracked and changes only by the edits given (Tasks 2, 3, 4, 6, 7). Run `cargo` with the lock as given (do not run `cargo update`).
- **No panic on any path a CLI user, MCP client or HTTP client can reach:** no `unwrap()`, `expect()`, `panic!`, `unreachable!`, `todo!`, `unimplemented!` outside test code (checked in Task 9 with `cargo clippy --workspace --lib --bins -- -A clippy::all -D clippy::unwrap_used -D clippy::expect_used -D clippy::panic -D clippy::unreachable -D clippy::todo -D clippy::unimplemented`). A panic in a server worker is caught, answered with 500 and logged as `internal` (E0819).
- Diagnostic codes only from the spec's ranges: E0240–E0249 (check), E0520–E0539 (runtime), E0800–E0819 (service). Used here: E0240 `reserved_name`, E0241 `sql_not_literal`, E0242 `sql_invalid`, E0243 `package_mismatch`, E0520 `bad_entry`, E0521 `bad_response`, E0522 `budget_exceeded`, E0523 `db_open`, E0524 `init_failed`, E0800 `request_rejected`, E0801 `overloaded`, E0802 `listen_failed`, E0819 `internal`. All messages English.
- Limits (spec §2.5) — per request: 5 s, 500 000 000 fuel, 64 MiB guest memory (max 256) = host budget, head 16 KiB / 64 headers, body 1 MiB, response 8 MiB, header read 5 s, body read 10 s, keep-alive 100 requests / 5 s idle, kept `print` output 4 KiB, 16 `net` calls, `fs` writes 64 MiB / 256 calls, 10 000 rows per query, 1 GiB database. `qal_run`/`qal_call` keep 10 s / 2·10⁹ fuel / 256 MiB and get the budgets.
- Builds and tests must be warning-free (`cargo build --workspace --all-targets`), every test binary must report `test result: ok.` (no SIGABRT).
- Performance gate (spec §2.2): mean instantiate+discard ≤ 200 µs for the service module in a release build (Task 6, re-run in Task 9).

## Review Focus

Inputs and failure modes the spec implies that are most likely to bite a person using this software, each pinned by a test (the task that owns the code):

1. **A handler that traps, loops or allocates a lot, then the next request** — must get a correct response from a fresh instance: Task 6 `every_request_runs_in_a_fresh_instance` (int overflow → 500, endless recursion → 503, ~32 MiB 20 times in a 40 MiB limit, CR/LF in a header → 500).
2. **A 4xx response after a write** — the write must not persist: Task 6 `writes_are_committed_only_for_successful_responses`, Task 7 `agents_test_services_with_qal_request` (`/fail` → `rollback`).
3. **Requests that try to desynchronize the parser** (CL+TE, two Content-Lengths, `+3`, folded headers, absolute-form targets, HTTP/2 preface, slowloris): refused and the connection closed — Task 6 `ambiguous_or_oversized_requests_are_refused`, `slow_clients_time_out`, `the_host_refuses_bad_requests_and_closes`.
4. **A program reaching private networks through names or IP spellings** (rebinding, `169.254.169.254`, `::ffff:10.0.0.1`, `2130706433`, redirects to metadata): denied — Task 4 `destinations_must_be_granted_and_public`, `redirects_are_returned_not_followed`.
5. **SQL text from data** (`"... " ++ t`, a variable, `; DROP`, `PRAGMA`, `ATTACH`, `VACUUM INTO`, named parameters): a check error before anything runs, plus the runtime authorizer — Task 1 `sql_must_be_a_valid_literal`, Task 3 `the_authorizer_refuses_control_statements_and_functions`.

Confirmed findings of the independent review, fixed in Task 9:

| # | Finding (severity) | Fix (Task 9) | Pinned by |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | **SQL ignored the wall-clock limit** (high): the epoch interrupts only wasm; a recursive CTE ran 9 s under a 300 ms limit while holding the write lock | `Db` has a progress handler (every 1 000 VM steps) that interrupts a statement past the execution's deadline (`Db::limit`, set per execution; cleared for the host's own COMMIT/ROLLBACK); `SQLITE_INTERRUPT` ends the execution with `timeout` (host failure kind `FAIL_TIMEOUT`) | `sql_statements_stop_at_the_wall_clock_limit` |
| F2 | **SQLite's own allocations bypassed the host budget** (high): `randomblob(600000000)` succeeded under a 16 MiB limit | `SQLITE_LIMIT_LENGTH` = the execution's memory limit (per execution), `SQLITE_LIMIT_SQL_LENGTH` = 64 KiB; rusqlite feature `limits` | `values_sqlite_builds_are_bounded_by_the_memory_limit` |
| F3/F4 | **TEMP tables escaped `max_page_count` and survived on a worker's connection** (medium): 20 MiB in a 1 MiB database; a later request saw an earlier one's TEMP table | the authorizer refuses `CREATE TEMP TABLE/INDEX/TRIGGER/VIEW` and writes to the `temp` schema | `temp_objects_are_refused` |
| F5 | **Decoded arguments were not charged** (medium): 1 M `SqlNull` parameters took 112 MiB of host heap under a 32 MiB limit | `decode_exact` counts 64 bytes per node (`NODE_BYTES`), host functions decode within what is left of the budget and charge the result; exceeding it is `out_of_memory` with `host: true` | `decoded_arguments_count_against_the_host_budget` |
| H1 | **Handlers could send 1xx as the final response** (medium): `101` + `upgrade` would switch a proxy to a tunnel | valid statuses are 200–599 (a deliberate narrowing of spec §2.6's 100–599), `upgrade` is a host-only header | `responses_are_validated` |
| H2 | **Idle keep-alive clients held every worker** (medium, design): one client sending a request every 250 ms blocked a 1-worker server for others | while connections wait in the queue, a kept-alive connection is closed after its current request (`Shared::pending`) | `kept_alive_clients_do_not_hold_workers_while_others_wait` |
| P1 | **An import could replace the project's own definitions** (medium): a package `lib` silently replaced the importer's `check_password` | an import may replace only names whose previous version came from the same package (`qal-<pkg>@…`); otherwise E0243 with "would replace the project's own `x`" | `packages_never_replace_the_projects_own_definitions` |
| F6 | **Hard links reached files outside** (low; needs a pre-existing link) | files with `st_nlink > 1` are refused like paths outside | `hard_links_to_files_outside_are_refused` |
| F7 | **SSRF blocklist gaps** (low): `::ffff:0:a.b.c.d` (SIIT), `fec0::/10`, Teredo `2001::/32`, `100::/64` | blocked (SIIT embeds IPv4 and is checked as such) | `private_and_reserved_ranges_are_blocked_for_names` |
| S | Smaller ones the reviewer suspected | `net` re-reads the time left after the DNS lookup; `try_proxy_from_env(false)` is explicit; two `Host` headers are 400; `qal_request` refuses a `content-length` header that does not match the body | `ambiguous_or_oversized_requests_are_refused` and the existing net tests |

Checked by the reviewer and found sound: URL normalization of IPv4 spellings, trailing dots, no rebinding (fixed resolver), no redirects or proxies, port binding, userinfo refusal; the literal-only SQL rule (built-ins are not values), rusqlite's multiple-statement check, pragma table-valued functions denied, defensive mode for schema writes; CL/TE smuggling, duplicate Content-Length, folded headers, non-UTF-8, absolute-form, Expect, HEAD, pipelining; no reachable panic. Left as documented limits: a hanging system resolver leaves its helper thread behind (bounded by the 16-calls budget per request); `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` may wait up to 5 s before the request's own deadline starts.

## Design Decisions

Decided here (the user delegates design decisions; the spec leaves these open). Each is reflected in the code below.

1. **Prelude types are built into the checker and referenced by name.** `qal_core::prelude::types()` defines the eight types of spec §2.1/§3 in Rust; the checker puts them in front of every program (so `TProgram::types` — and with it every compiled module — carries their layout), and a user type or variant with a prelude name is `reserved_name` (E0240, also for `rename`). In canonical forms a prelude type is the new reference tag `R_PRELUDE = 2` followed by its name (`ref := 0 hash | 1 group-index | 2 prelude-name`): prelude types have no definition to hash, are not stored dependencies, and existing hashes do not change (canonical `VERSION` stays 1). The decoder accepts only prelude names and checks that a prelude variant belongs to its prelude enum.
2. **SQL literals are checked by a small SQLite-compatible tokenizer in `qal-core::sql`** (not by a schema-less SQLite): strings, quoted identifiers, `--`/`/* */` comments, parameters; exactly one statement (one trailing `;`; a `CREATE TRIGGER` body may hold `;` and ends at `; END ;`, like `sqlite3_complete`); only `?` (no `?NNN`, `:x`, `@x`, `$x`, `#x`); first keyword (also after `EXPLAIN [QUERY PLAN]`) not one of ATTACH, DETACH, PRAGMA, VACUUM, BEGIN, COMMIT, END, ROLLBACK, SAVEPOINT, RELEASE; at most 64 KiB, no NUL. The checker runs it on every literal (E0242) and the host again before `prepare`. E0241 when the argument is not a literal; built-ins are not values, so no wrapper can pass SQL through.
3. **The authorizer** denies ATTACH, DETACH, PRAGMA, transaction and savepoint actions, unknown actions, and the functions `load_extension`, `readfile`, `writefile`, `fts3_tokenizer`, `edit`; from Task 9 also every TEMP object (`CREATE TEMP …`, writes to `temp`: they would outlive the execution on a worker's connection and are not bounded by `max_page_count`); it is set only while a guest statement is prepared and runs, so the host's own `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/pragmas are unaffected. It does **not** filter writes to `sqlite_master`: SQLite reports CREATE/ALTER/DROP's internal schema writes as the same INSERT/UPDATE actions without an accessor, so such a rule would forbid `CREATE TABLE`. Direct schema writes are refused by SQLite itself (no `writable_schema`, `SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE` on); a test pins that. Connections also get `trusted_schema=OFF`, `cell_size_check=ON`, `foreign_keys=ON`, WAL, `busy_timeout` 5 s, `page_size` 4096 and `max_page_count` from `--db-max-mib`; files are opened with `SQLITE_OPEN_NOFOLLOW` after canonicalizing the directory (so only a link at the file itself is refused, not `/var -> /private/var`).
4. **One transaction per execution, owned by the host.** `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` before the entry point if its effects contain `db`; commit for a handler result that validates with status 100–399, `init` returning `Ok(())`, `qal_run` exit code 0 (`Runner::run_entry`), `qal_call` without runtime error (`Runner::invoke`); otherwise rollback. If SQLite rolled the transaction back by itself (e.g. `SQLITE_FULL`), later statements of that execution fail with `DbSql` instead of running outside it, and a requested commit becomes a runtime error. Results report `db: "commit" | "rollback"` (`qal_run`/`qal_call` output and `qal_request`), `none` when no database was used. `BEGIN` timing out is `timeout` (→ 503). From Task 9 a progress handler interrupts a statement at the execution's deadline (also `timeout`), and `SQLITE_LIMIT_LENGTH` is the execution's memory limit.
5. **Database location.** `db` → `<project>/.qal/db/main.sqlite`, `db:<name>` (`[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,31}`) → `<name>.sqlite`, `db:mem` fresh in memory; the project is the store's (CLI: the nearest `.qal/store.db` around the working directory, via `qal_store::project_of`; MCP: the root). `qal serve` refuses `db:mem` (a server needs a shared file). At most one database grant per execution.
6. **`net` grants and SSRF.** `net:<host>[:port]` with lowercase DNS names (refused: uppercase, names whose last label is numeric or `0x…` — the URL parser would read them as IPv4 — and `localhost`/`*.localhost`), `*.domain` (two labels at least; only subdomains), IPv4 or `[IPv6]` literals. Without a port only 80 (http) and 443 (https). URLs are parsed with the `url` crate (WHATWG: `2130706433`, `0x7f000001`, `017700000001`, `127.1` all become `127.0.0.1`); an IP host needs an IP grant of the same address (IPv4-mapped IPv6 counts as its IPv4) and is never range-checked (deliberate operator choice); a name is resolved in a helper thread (bounded by the time left), every address is checked, and ureq connects through a resolver that returns exactly those addresses. Blocked for names: spec §5.1 plus IPv4 embedded in IPv4-compatible (`::a.b.c.d`), NAT64 (`64:ff9b::/96`, `64:ff9b:1::/48`) and 6to4 (`2002::/16`) addresses. No redirects (3xx are returned), no proxies (ureq's `proxy-from-env` feature is not enabled), `connection: close`, `user-agent: qal/<version>`; guest headers `host`, `content-length`, `connection`, `user-agent`, `transfer-encoding`, non-token names and CR/LF/NUL values are `NetInvalid`. Connect 5 s, total 10 s (never more than the execution has left), body ≤ 4 MiB, UTF-8.
7. **Host functions build whole values in guest memory.** `net.http(call: i32) -> i32`, `db.exec(sql, params) -> i32`, `db.query(sql, params) -> i32`: the host reads the arguments exactly (`value::decode_exact`: no shortened strings, valid UTF-8, bounded by the memory limit), runs the operation and writes the `Result` with `guest::Guest::encode` through the module's own `qal_alloc` (so `out_of_memory` applies). The same encoder passes records and enums as call arguments (`Runner::encode`).
8. **`fs` through descriptors** (spec §5.3): `FsRoot` holds the granted directory open (`O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC`) from the moment the grant is parsed; guest paths are resolved lexically and opened component by component with `openat(…, O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW)`, the last one with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK` (write: `O_CREAT|O_WRONLY`, 0o644, truncated after `fstat` says regular file). A granted directory is reachable by its canonical path and by the absolute path it was granted as (macOS `/var` vs `/private/var`). Phase 1's `check_path`/`verify_opened`/`open_nofollow` and their unit tests are replaced by `fs.rs` tests for the same behaviour plus the directory-swap race; the behaviour tests in `qal-run/src/tests.rs` stay green unchanged. `fs` is Unix-only (as in Phase 1).
9. **Host budget = memory limit** (spec §5.4): kept output, `read_line`/`read_all` input, file contents, `net` response bodies and headers, query results (strings + 8 bytes per value), a request's head and body (`ExecIn::host_used`) and (Task 9) the host's decoded copies of guest arguments (64 bytes per value node) are charged; exceeding it is `out_of_memory` with `values.host = true` and a hint. Budgets are traps `budget_exceeded` (E0522) with `values {budget, used, kind}` for `fs_bytes`, `fs_writes`, `net_calls`. `Limits` carries them (and `db_max_rows`, `db_max_mib`).
10. **Prepared runners** (`Runner::prepared`): the pooling allocator with `workers + 2` slots, `max_memory_size` = request memory, one table of `max(1, functions)` elements; if the pool cannot be created, the on-demand allocator (the log line `listening` says `"pooling": false`). The module is linked once (`InstancePre`); `Runner::execute` runs on the calling thread (workers have `GUEST_THREAD_STACK`), creates a store, instantiates, runs, and drops everything. An epoch ticker increments the epoch every 10 ms; each store's deadline is `ceil(timeout / 10 ms) + 1` ticks. Phase 1's watchdog stays for `Runner::new`.
11. **Service pipeline** (`qal_serve::Service`): handler and init signatures are checked before start (`bad_entry`, with expected and found signature in the message), and the effects of both must be granted (`permission_denied` before start). The module is compiled from `Entry::Fns([handler, init])` (new in `qal-wasm`). Valid response: status 100–599 until Task 9, then **200–599** (review finding H1: a final 1xx desynchronizes clients and proxies; a deliberate narrowing of spec §2.6); header names tokens; values without CR/LF/NUL; no `content-length`/`transfer-encoding`/`connection` (Task 9: nor `upgrade`); body ≤ max; **204 and 304 (and 1xx) must have an empty body and are sent without `content-length`**; a handler's `x-request-id` is replaced by the host's. Default `content-type: text/plain; charset=utf-8` when the handler sets none. Results are decoded exactly up to max response + 1 MiB (`with_exact_results`). Errors: `timeout` → 503 `{"error":{"kind":"timeout","id":…}}`, everything else → 500 `internal_error`; `--debug-errors` puts the full runtime error in the body. `init` returning `Err(m)` is `init_failed` (E0524, a code the spec leaves open).
12. **HTTP layer.** Host rejections are answered with a JSON body and `connection: close` (no resync after an error). HTTP/1.1 needs `Host` (400). `Expect: 100-continue` gets `100 Continue` before the body is read (413 first if too large); other expectations are ignored. Header deadline: 5 s from when a worker takes the connection (time in the queue does not count against a client that sent promptly) or from the first byte of a later request on a kept-alive connection, which may idle 5 s; 408 with a JSON body when it passes. Pipelined requests are answered in order.
13. **Server** (`qal_serve::serve`): synchronous; accept thread with a `sync_channel(queue)`; a full queue is answered `503` + `Retry-After: 1` from the accept thread (1 s write timeout). Shutdown: SIGINT/SIGTERM (signal-hook) set a flag; a helper thread wakes the blocking `accept`; queued and running requests finish (kept-alive connections close after their current request) for up to 10 s; the CLI then logs `{"event":"stopped","served":n}` and exits 0. Start failures (bad handler, missing grant, database, init, bind) are one line `{"event":"start_failed","error":…}` on the log and exit 1; option errors are usage errors (exit 2). Request ids are a process-wide counter (`x-request-id`).
14. **`qal_request` / `qal request`** run `Service::handle` for each request without TCP (same limits, same validation, same transaction rule; one `Service` and one worker per call, so `db:mem` is shared by the call's requests and `init` runs first). Request objects: `method` (default GET, a token), `path` (starting with `/`, no `?` or whitespace), `query`, `headers` (names lowercased, hop-by-hop dropped, as the HTTP layer does), `body` or `json` (serialized, `content-type: application/json` added; `json: null` means no body); `content-length` is added for a non-empty body; at most 50; bodies over 1 MiB get the host's 413. Results: `{status, headers, body, json?, db, stdout?, error?}`. A failing request (500/503) is data, not a tool error; start errors are `error`. `openWorldHint: true` (static annotations cannot depend on the grants; the same now holds for `qal_run`/`qal_call`, which can reach the network). The MCP permission hint names `qal_request` too and turns `db` into `"db:mem"`.
15. **Packages** (spec §4.3, needed by 3b/3c): `qal export <prefix> --out <file> [--name <pkg>]` exports the names with the prefix plus their dependencies; the package name defaults to the file stem (`std/std.qalpkg` → `std`); `# def` lines sorted by name with the agent of the last `added`/`replaced` history entry; text in dependency order (ties and recursive groups by name), definitions separated by blank lines; checksum = SHA-256 of everything after the `# package` line. `qal import <file>` defines the text in one `define` with agent `qal-<pkg>@<hash prefix>` and commits only if every listed name got its recorded hash and nothing unlisted was defined (`package_mismatch`, E0243; a bad checksum fails before anything is written), recording `meta["package:<name>"]`. E0244 (`std_signature_change`), `meta.role`, the `std.` namespace and auto-import are Phase 3b.
16. **Store schema 3**: version 3 adds `meta (key, value)`; a version-2 store is migrated when opened (one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` that only creates `meta`). The embedded `qal_cli::STD_PACKAGE` (`include_str!("../../../std/std.qalpkg")`) is the empty package (headers only); nothing imports it yet.
17. **Language reference**: a 10-line "Services" section (prelude types, handler signature, `db_exec`/`db_query` with the literal rule, the transaction rule, `http`, grants, `qal_query name "std."`); the reference may now have 57 lines / 8000 bytes (was 45 / 6000).

Carried-over items (roadmap "Phase 3") and where they are done: `fs` writes through a verified parent descriptor, no empty file outside (Task 2, Decision 8); host memory as a whole coupled to the limit (Tasks 2–4, 6, Decision 9).

## Performance Gate (spec §2.2)

Measured by `instance_overhead_meets_the_gate` (Task 6; run again in Task 9) with the service module of `qal/crates/qal-serve/tests/fixtures/app.qal` (a todo service with database, JSON building and the misbehaving endpoints — the stand-in for the 3c todo module): mean time of `Runner::instantiate_only` (fresh store, instantiate from the `InstancePre`, drop) over 2 000 runs after 100 warm-up runs, release build, Apple M1, pooling allocator active: **10.783µs** — far below 200 µs. The fallback of the spec (one instance per worker with heap reset and zero fill) is therefore **not** implemented. In debug builds the test only requires ≤ 2 ms.

## File Structure

| File | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `qal/crates/qal-core/src/prelude.rs` | The prelude types, reserved names |
| `qal/crates/qal-core/src/sql.rs` | Static check of SQL literals |
| `qal/crates/qal-core/src/{canon,tast,diag,lib}.rs` | `R_PRELUDE` references; built-ins `http`, `db_exec`, `db_query`; E0240–E0242 |
| `qal/crates/qal-check/src/{lib,builtins}.rs` | Prelude in every program, reserved names, built-in types, SQL literal rule |
| `qal/crates/qal-wasm/src/{lib,gen}.rs` | Imports `net.http`, `db.exec`, `db.query`; `Entry::Fns` |
| `qal/crates/qal-run/src/fs.rs` | `fs` through directory descriptors (`openat`) |
| `qal/crates/qal-run/src/guest.rs` | Writing any value into guest memory |
| `qal/crates/qal-run/src/db.rs` | SQLite connection, authorizer, statements, transaction |
| `qal/crates/qal-run/src/net.rs` | Grants, SSRF checks, the HTTP client |
| `qal/crates/qal-run/src/{host,lib,value}.rs` | Permissions, budgets, host functions; transaction rule; prepared runner; exact decoding |
| `qal/crates/qal-serve/` (new) | `service.rs` pipeline, `http.rs` parser/writer, `server.rs` workers and log; tests with `tests/fixtures/app.qal` |
| `qal/crates/qal-cli/src/{main,ops,mcp,lib}.rs`, `reference.md` | `qal serve`, `qal request`, `qal export`, `qal import`, MCP `qal_request`, Services reference, `STD_PACKAGE` |

### Task 1: Prelude types, SQL literal check, `http`/`db_exec`/`db_query` in checker and code generator

**Files:**
- Create: `qal/crates/qal-core/src/prelude.rs`, `qal/crates/qal-core/src/sql.rs`
- Modify: `qal/crates/qal-core/src/{lib,tast,diag,canon}.rs`, `qal/crates/qal-check/src/{lib,builtins}.rs`, `qal/crates/qal-wasm/src/{lib,gen}.rs`
- Test: inline modules of `prelude.rs`, `sql.rs`, `canon.rs`, `tast.rs`; `qal/crates/qal-check/src/tests.rs`, `qal/crates/qal-wasm/tests/codegen.rs`, `qal/crates/qal-store/src/tests.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `qal_core::prelude::{TYPE_NAMES, types() -> Vec<TypeDef>, is_type, variant_owner(&str) -> Option<&'static str>, is_reserved}`; `qal_core::sql::{check_statement(&str) -> Result<usize, String>, FORBIDDEN, MAX_SQL_BYTES}`; `Diagnostic::{reserved_name(def, path, name), sql_not_literal(def, path, callee), sql_invalid(def, path, reason)}`; `Builtin::{Http, DbExec, DbQuery}` with effects `net`/`db`; `KNOWN_EFFECTS` += `net`, `db`; `qal_wasm::host::{NET_HTTP = "net.http", DB_EXEC = "db.exec", DB_QUERY = "db.query"}` (appended to `EFFECT_FNS`, now 10); canonical tag `R_PRELUDE = 2`.
- Every `TProgram` from `qal_check::check` now starts with the 8 prelude types.

#[test]
fn prelude_types_exist_in_every_program() {
    let src = "fn ok(rq: HttpRequest) -> HttpResponse = HttpResponse { status: 200, headers: [HttpHeader { name: \"x-path\", value: rq.path }], body: rq.body }\n\
               fn code(e: NetError) -> Int = match e { NetDenied(_) => 1, NetInvalid(m) => len(m), NetConnect(_) => 3, NetTimeout => 4, NetTooLarge => 5 }\n\
               fn cell(v: SqlValue) -> Str = match v { SqlNull => \"null\", SqlInt(n) => from_int(n), SqlFloat(x) => from_float(x), SqlText(s) => s }\n\
               fn rows(r: DbRows) -> Int = len(r.rows) + len(r.columns)\n\
               fn busy(e: DbError) -> Bool = match e { DbBusy => true, _ => false }\n\
               fn call() -> HttpCall = HttpCall { method: \"GET\", url: \"http://127.0.0.1:9/\", headers: [], body: \"\" }";
    assert_eq!(check_src(src), vec![]);
    let tp = typed(src);
    assert_eq!(&tp.types[..8].iter().map(|t| t.name.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), &qal_core::prelude::TYPE_NAMES);
    let d = one("fn f(e: DbError) -> Int = match e { DbBusy => 1 }");
    assert_eq!(d.kind, "non_exhaustive_match");
}

#[test]
fn prelude_names_are_reserved() {
    for src in ["type HttpRequest = { a: Int }", "type SqlValue = A | B", "type E = SqlInt(Int) | Other", "type F = DbBusy"] {
        let d = one(src);
        assert_eq!((d.code, d.kind), ("E0240", "reserved_name"), "{src}");
    }
    assert_eq!(one("type E = A | SqlInt(Int)").path, "variants[1]");
}

#[test]
fn net_and_db_builtins_need_their_effects() {
    let ok = "fn fetch(u: Str) -> Result[HttpResponse, NetError] !net = http(HttpCall { method: \"GET\", url: u, headers: [], body: \"\" })\n\
              fn add(t: Str) -> Result[Int, DbError] !db = db_exec(\"INSERT INTO todos (title) VALUES (?)\", [SqlText(t)])\n\
              fn all() -> Result[DbRows, DbError] !db = db_query(\"SELECT * FROM todos\", [])";
    assert_eq!(check_src(ok), vec![]);
    assert_eq!(kinds("fn f() -> Result[DbRows, DbError] = db_query(\"SELECT 1\", [])"), vec![("effect_violation", "f".into(), "body".into())]);
    let d = one("fn f(c: HttpCall) -> Bool = match http(c) { Ok(_) => true, Err(_) => false }");
    assert_eq!((d.kind, d.message.contains("net")), ("effect_violation", true));
    assert_eq!(one("fn f() -> Result[Int, DbError] !db = db_exec(\"DELETE FROM t\", [1])").kind, "type_mismatch");
}

#[test]
fn sql_must_be_a_valid_literal() {
    let d = one("fn f(sql: Str) -> Result[Int, DbError] !db = db_exec(sql, [])");
    assert_eq!((d.code, d.kind, d.path.as_str()), ("E0241", "sql_not_literal", "body.args[0]"));
    assert!(d.hints[0].contains("`?` placeholders"), "{:?}", d.hints);
    let d = one("fn f(t: Str) -> Result[DbRows, DbError] !db = db_query(\"SELECT * FROM \" ++ t, [])");
    assert_eq!(d.code, "E0241");
    for (sql, why) in [("SELECT 1; DROP TABLE t", "only one statement"), ("SELECT * FROM t WHERE id = :id", "named parameters"), ("PRAGMA journal_mode = DELETE", "not allowed"), ("ATTACH '/etc/passwd' AS p", "not allowed"), ("COMMIT", "not allowed"), ("SELECT 'x", "unterminated")] {
        let d = one(&format!("fn f() -> Result[Int, DbError] !db = db_exec({}, [])", qal_text::printer::print_str(sql)));
        assert_eq!((d.code, d.kind), ("E0242", "sql_invalid"), "{sql}");
        assert!(d.message.contains(why), "{sql}: {}", d.message);
    }
}

    #[test]
    fn prelude_types_are_referenced_by_name() {
        let r = Def::Fn(FnDef {
            name: "f".into(),
            params: vec![Param { name: "req".into(), ty: Type::Named("HttpRequest".into()) }],
            ret: Type::Named("SqlValue".into()),
            effects: vec![],
            req: vec![],
            ens: vec![],
            body: Expr::Call("SqlText".into(), vec![Expr::Field(var("req"), "path".into())]),
        });
        let h = Def::Fn(FnDef {
            name: "h".into(),
            params: vec![],
            ret: Type::Named("HttpHeader".into()),
            effects: vec![],
            req: vec![],
            ens: vec![],
            body: Expr::Record("HttpHeader".into(), vec![("name".into(), Expr::Str("a".into())), ("value".into(), Expr::Str("b".into()))]),
        });
        let p = Program { defs: vec![r.clone(), h.clone()] };
        let c = by_name(&p);
        for (name, def) in [("f", &r), ("h", &h)] {
            assert!(c[name].deps.is_empty() && c[name].refs.is_empty(), "prelude types are not dependencies");
            assert_eq!(references_of(single(&c[name])).unwrap(), vec![]);
            let back = decode(single(&c[name]), name, &c[name].local_names, &mut |_| "?".into()).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(&back, def);
        }
        let needle = |s: &str| [&[R_PRELUDE, s.len() as u8][..], s.as_bytes()].concat();
        assert!(c["f"].canonical.windows(13).any(|w| w == needle("HttpRequest").as_slice()));
        // A prelude variant under another prelude enum, or an unknown prelude name, does not decode.
        let swap = |bytes: &[u8], from: &str, to: &str| {
            let at = bytes.windows(from.len()).rposition(|w| w == from.as_bytes()).unwrap();
            let mut out = bytes.to_vec();
            out[at..at + from.len()].copy_from_slice(to.as_bytes());
            out
        };
        let wrong_owner = swap(&c["f"].canonical, "SqlValue", "NetError");
        assert!(decode(Form::new(&wrong_owner, None), "f", &c["f"].local_names, &mut |_| "?".into()).unwrap_err().contains("does not belong"));
        let unknown = swap(&c["h"].canonical, "HttpHeader", "HttpHeadeX");
        assert!(decode(Form::new(&unknown, None), "h", &[], &mut |_| "?".into()).unwrap_err().contains("not a prelude type"));
    }
```

#[test]
fn definitions_using_prelude_types_store_and_view() {
    let mut s = store();
    let src = "fn handle(rq: HttpRequest) -> HttpResponse = HttpResponse { status: 200, headers: [HttpHeader { name: \"x\", value: rq.path }], body: \"\" }\n\
               fn cell(v: SqlValue) -> Int = match v { SqlInt(n) => n, _ => 0 }";
    let d = ok(&mut s, src);
    assert_eq!(statuses(&d), vec![("handle", "added"), ("cell", "added")]);
    assert_eq!(count(&s, "deps"), 0, "prelude types are not stored dependencies");
    assert_eq!(text(&mut s, "handle"), "fn handle(rq: HttpRequest) -> HttpResponse\n= HttpResponse { status: 200, headers: [HttpHeader { name: \"x\", value: rq.path }], body: \"\" }");
    assert_eq!(statuses(&ok(&mut s, src)), vec![("handle", "unchanged"), ("cell", "unchanged")]);
    let e = err(&mut s, "type DbRows = { n: Int }");
    assert_eq!(e[0].code, "E0240");
    // A stored type cannot be renamed to a prelude name either.
    ok(&mut s, "type Row = { n: Int }");
    assert_eq!(s.rename("Row", "DbRows", "test").unwrap_err()[0].code, "E0240");
}
```

#[test]
fn net_and_db_calls_import_their_host_functions() {
    let m = build(
        "fn fetch(c: HttpCall) -> Result[HttpResponse, NetError] !net = http(c)\n\
         fn add(t: Str) -> Result[Int, DbError] !db = db_exec(\"INSERT INTO t (a) VALUES (?)\", [SqlText(t)])\n\
         fn all() -> Result[DbRows, DbError] !db = db_query(\"SELECT a FROM t\", [])\n\
         fn main(args: List[Str]) -> Int !net !db = match all() { Ok(r) => len(r.rows), Err(_) => match add(\"x\") { Ok(n) => n, Err(_) => 1 } }",
        Entry::All,
    );
    assert_eq!(m.imports, vec![host::FAIL, host::NET_HTTP, host::DB_EXEC, host::DB_QUERY]);
    assert_eq!(host::signature(host::DB_QUERY), Some((&[host::ValType::I32, host::ValType::I32][..], &[host::ValType::I32][..])));
    assert_eq!(host::effect(host::NET_HTTP), Some("net"));
    // The prelude types travel with the module (the host decodes and encodes them).
    assert!(m.types.iter().any(|t| t.name == "HttpResponse") && m.types.iter().any(|t| t.name == "SqlValue"));
    wasmparser::Validator::new().validate_all(&m.wasm).unwrap();
}

    /// The SQL text of `db_exec`/`db_query` is part of the program: a string literal that passes the
    /// static check (one statement, `?` parameters, no database or transaction control).
    fn sql_literal(&mut self, callee: &str, arg: &Expr, path: &str) {
        match arg {
            Expr::Str(sql) => {
                if let Err(reason) = qal_core::sql::check_statement(sql) {
                    self.diags.push(Diagnostic::sql_invalid(self.def, path, &reason));
                }
            }
            _ => self.diags.push(Diagnostic::sql_not_literal(self.def, path, callee)),
        }
    }

fn globals(p: &Program, diags: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) -> Globals {
    let mut g = Globals { tp: TProgram::default(), fns: HashMap::new(), variants: HashMap::new() };
    // The prelude types come first; they exist in every program.
    for t in prelude::types() {
        if let TypeBody::Enum(vs) = &t.body {
            for (tag, v) in vs.iter().enumerate() {
                g.variants.insert(v.name.clone(), (t.name.clone(), tag as u32, v.fields.clone()));
            }
        }
        g.tp.types.push(t);
    }
    for d in &p.defs {
        let Def::Type(t) = d else { continue };
        if prelude::is_reserved(&t.name) {
            diags.push(Diagnostic::reserved_name(&t.name, "", &t.name));
            continue;
        }
        if Type::BUILTIN_NAMES.contains(&t.name.as_str()) {
            diags.push(Diagnostic::duplicate_name(&t.name, "", &t.name).hint(format!("`{}` is a built-in type", t.name)));
```

//! QAL core: the program graph (AST), the typed program, canonical form and hashes, structured
//! diagnostics, the prelude types and the static SQL check.

Create `qal/crates/qal-core/src/prelude.rs`:

````rust
//! The prelude: built-in record and enum types for services (Phase 3 spec §2.1, §3.1, §3.2).
//!
//! Like `Option` and `Result`, these types exist in every program without being defined: their
//! names and variant names are reserved, and the host knows their layout (declaration order).
//! In canonical forms they are referenced by name, never by hash (see `canon`).

use crate::ast::{Type, TypeBody, TypeDef, Variant};

/// The prelude type names, in declaration order.
pub const TYPE_NAMES: [&str; 8] = ["HttpHeader", "HttpRequest", "HttpResponse", "HttpCall", "NetError", "SqlValue", "DbRows", "DbError"];

fn named(n: &str) -> Type {
    Type::Named(n.to_string())
}

fn record(name: &str, fields: &[(&str, Type)]) -> TypeDef {
    TypeDef { name: name.into(), body: TypeBody::Record(fields.iter().map(|(f, t)| (f.to_string(), t.clone())).collect()) }
}

fn enumeration(name: &str, variants: &[(&str, &[Type])]) -> TypeDef {
    TypeDef { name: name.into(), body: TypeBody::Enum(variants.iter().map(|(v, fs)| Variant { name: v.to_string(), fields: fs.to_vec() }).collect()) }
}

/// The prelude type definitions:
///
/// ```text
/// type HttpHeader   = { name: Str, value: Str }
/// type HttpRequest  = { method: Str, path: Str, query: Str, headers: List[HttpHeader], body: Str }
/// type HttpResponse = { status: Int, headers: List[HttpHeader], body: Str }
/// type HttpCall     = { method: Str, url: Str, headers: List[HttpHeader], body: Str }
/// type NetError     = NetDenied(Str) | NetInvalid(Str) | NetConnect(Str) | NetTimeout | NetTooLarge
/// type SqlValue     = SqlNull | SqlInt(Int) | SqlFloat(Float) | SqlText(Str)
/// type DbRows       = { columns: List[Str], rows: List[List[SqlValue]] }
/// type DbError      = DbConstraint(Str) | DbSql(Str) | DbBusy | DbFull | DbTooLarge
/// ```
pub fn types() -> Vec<TypeDef> {
    let headers = Type::list(named("HttpHeader"));
    vec![
        record("HttpHeader", &[("name", Type::Str), ("value", Type::Str)]),
        record("HttpRequest", &[("method", Type::Str), ("path", Type::Str), ("query", Type::Str), ("headers", headers.clone()), ("body", Type::Str)]),
        record("HttpResponse", &[("status", Type::Int), ("headers", headers.clone()), ("body", Type::Str)]),
        record("HttpCall", &[("method", Type::Str), ("url", Type::Str), ("headers", headers), ("body", Type::Str)]),
        enumeration("NetError", &[("NetDenied", &[Type::Str]), ("NetInvalid", &[Type::Str]), ("NetConnect", &[Type::Str]), ("NetTimeout", &[]), ("NetTooLarge", &[])]),
        enumeration("SqlValue", &[("SqlNull", &[]), ("SqlInt", &[Type::Int]), ("SqlFloat", &[Type::Float]), ("SqlText", &[Type::Str])]),
        record("DbRows", &[("columns", Type::list(Type::Str)), ("rows", Type::list(Type::list(named("SqlValue"))))]),
        enumeration("DbError", &[("DbConstraint", &[Type::Str]), ("DbSql", &[Type::Str]), ("DbBusy", &[]), ("DbFull", &[]), ("DbTooLarge", &[])]),
    ]
}

/// True if `name` is a prelude type.
pub fn is_type(name: &str) -> bool {
    TYPE_NAMES.contains(&name)
}

/// The prelude enum a variant name belongs to.
pub fn variant_owner(name: &str) -> Option<&'static str> {
    Some(match name {
        "NetDenied" | "NetInvalid" | "NetConnect" | "NetTimeout" | "NetTooLarge" => "NetError",
        "SqlNull" | "SqlInt" | "SqlFloat" | "SqlText" => "SqlValue",
        "DbConstraint" | "DbSql" | "DbBusy" | "DbFull" | "DbTooLarge" => "DbError",
        _ => return None,
    })
}

/// True if `name` is a prelude type or a variant of a prelude enum (reserved in user code).
pub fn is_reserved(name: &str) -> bool {
    is_type(name) || variant_owner(name).is_some()
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn names_and_variants_match_the_definitions() {
        let ts = types();
        assert_eq!(ts.iter().map(|t| t.name.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), TYPE_NAMES.to_vec());
        for t in &ts {
            assert!(is_type(&t.name) && is_reserved(&t.name));
            if let TypeBody::Enum(vs) = &t.body {
                for v in vs {
                    assert_eq!(variant_owner(&v.name), Some(TYPE_NAMES.iter().copied().find(|n| *n == t.name).unwrap()), "{}", v.name);
                }
            }
        }
        let variants: usize = ts.iter().map(|t| if let TypeBody::Enum(vs) = &t.body { vs.len() } else { 0 }).sum();
        assert_eq!(variants, 14);
        assert!(!is_reserved("Point") && variant_owner("Some").is_none() && !is_type("Option"));
    }
}
````

Create `qal/crates/qal-core/src/sql.rs`:

```rust
//! Static checks of the SQL literals of `db_exec` / `db_query` (Phase 3 spec §3.2).
//!
//! A small SQLite-compatible tokenizer (strings, quoted identifiers, comments, parameters) decides
//! without any schema: the text is exactly one complete statement (one trailing `;` allowed), uses
//! only positional `?` parameters, and does not start with a keyword that controls the database or
//! the transaction. The checker runs this on every literal (`sql_invalid`, E0242), and the host
//! runs it again before preparing a statement.

/// Statement keywords the guest may not use: attaching files, pragmas, vacuuming (`VACUUM INTO`
/// writes files) and transaction control (the host owns the one transaction per execution).
pub const FORBIDDEN: [&str; 10] = ["ATTACH", "DETACH", "PRAGMA", "VACUUM", "BEGIN", "COMMIT", "END", "ROLLBACK", "SAVEPOINT", "RELEASE"];

/// Longest accepted SQL text in bytes.
pub const MAX_SQL_BYTES: usize = 64 << 10;

#[derive(Debug, PartialEq)]
enum Tok {
    Word(String),
    Param,
    Semicolon,
    Other,
}

fn is_ident(c: char) -> bool {
    c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_' || c == '$' || !c.is_ascii()
}

/// Tokens of `sql`, without whitespace and comments.
fn tokens(sql: &str) -> Result<Vec<Tok>, String> {
    let cs: Vec<char> = sql.chars().collect();
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    let mut i = 0;
    let close = |cs: &[char], mut i: usize, q: char, what: &str| -> Result<usize, String> {
        // Doubling the quote escapes it (`'it''s'`).
        loop {
            match cs.get(i) {
                None => return Err(format!("unterminated {what}")),
                Some(c) if *c == q && cs.get(i + 1) == Some(&q) => i += 2,
                Some(c) if *c == q => return Ok(i + 1),
                Some(_) => i += 1,
            }
        }
    };
    while i < cs.len() {
        let c = cs[i];
        match c {
            _ if c.is_whitespace() => i += 1,
            '-' if cs.get(i + 1) == Some(&'-') => {
                while i < cs.len() && cs[i] != '\n' {
                    i += 1;
                }
            }
            '/' if cs.get(i + 1) == Some(&'*') => {
                let mut j = i + 2;
                loop {
                    match cs.get(j) {
                        None => return Err("unterminated comment".into()),
                        Some('*') if cs.get(j + 1) == Some(&'/') => break,
                        Some(_) => j += 1,
                    }
                }
                i = j + 2;
            }
            '\'' => {
                i = close(&cs, i + 1, '\'', "string")?;
                out.push(Tok::Other);
            }
            '"' | '`' => {
                i = close(&cs, i + 1, c, "quoted identifier")?;
                out.push(Tok::Other);
            }
            '[' => {
                let j = cs[i..].iter().position(|c| *c == ']').ok_or("unterminated quoted identifier")?;
                i += j + 1;
                out.push(Tok::Other);
            }
            ';' => {
                out.push(Tok::Semicolon);
                i += 1;
            }
            '?' => {
                if cs.get(i + 1).is_some_and(char::is_ascii_digit) {
                    return Err("numbered parameters (`?NNN`) are not allowed: use `?` and pass the values in order".into());
                }
                out.push(Tok::Param);
                i += 1;
            }
            ':' | '@' | '$' | '#' if cs.get(i + 1).is_some_and(|c| is_ident(*c)) => {
                return Err(format!("named parameters (`{c}name`) are not allowed: use `?` and pass the values in order"));
            }
            _ if is_ident(c) => {
                let start = i;
                while i < cs.len() && is_ident(cs[i]) {
                    i += 1;
                }
                out.push(Tok::Word(cs[start..i].iter().collect()));
            }
            _ => {
                out.push(Tok::Other);
                i += 1;
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(out)
}

fn word(t: Option<&Tok>) -> Option<String> {
    match t {
        Some(Tok::Word(w)) => Some(w.to_ascii_uppercase()),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// Index of the `;` that ends the first statement (or the number of tokens). As in
/// `sqlite3_complete`, a `CREATE [TEMP] TRIGGER` statement holds `;`s in its body and ends only
/// at `; END ;` (or at the end of the text).
fn statement_end(toks: &[Tok]) -> usize {
    let mut i = 0;
    if word(toks.get(i)).as_deref() == Some("CREATE") {
        i += 1;
        if matches!(word(toks.get(i)).as_deref(), Some("TEMP" | "TEMPORARY")) {
            i += 1;
        }
    }
    let trigger = i > 0 && word(toks.get(i)).as_deref() == Some("TRIGGER");
    for (k, t) in toks.iter().enumerate() {
        if *t != Tok::Semicolon {
            continue;
        }
        if !trigger || (k >= 2 && word(toks.get(k - 1)).as_deref() == Some("END") && toks[k - 2] == Tok::Semicolon) {
            return k;
        }
    }
    toks.len()
}

/// Checks one SQL literal; returns the number of `?` parameters, or why the text is refused.
pub fn check_statement(sql: &str) -> Result<usize, String> {
    if sql.len() > MAX_SQL_BYTES {
        return Err(format!("the SQL text is longer than {MAX_SQL_BYTES} bytes"));
    }
    if sql.contains('\0') {
        return Err("the SQL text contains a NUL character".into());
    }
    let toks = tokens(sql)?;
    let end = statement_end(&toks);
    if toks.len() > end + 1 {
        return Err("only one statement is allowed (a single trailing `;` is fine)".into());
    }
    let stmt = &toks[..end];
    let mut words = stmt.iter().map(|t| match t {
        Tok::Word(w) => Some(w.to_ascii_uppercase()),
        _ => None,
    });
    let mut first = words.next().flatten().ok_or("the SQL text has no statement")?;
    // `EXPLAIN [QUERY PLAN] <stmt>` is judged by the explained statement.
    if first == "EXPLAIN" {
        let mut next = words.next().flatten().unwrap_or_default();
        if next == "QUERY" {
            words.next();
            next = words.next().flatten().unwrap_or_default();
        }
        first = next;
    }
    if FORBIDDEN.contains(&first.as_str()) {
        return Err(format!("`{first}` statements are not allowed: the host controls the database file, its settings and the transaction"));
    }
    Ok(stmt.iter().filter(|t| **t == Tok::Param).count())
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn accepts_one_statement_with_positional_parameters() {
        assert_eq!(check_statement("SELECT id, title FROM todos WHERE id = ?"), Ok(1));
        assert_eq!(check_statement("insert into t (a, b) values (?, ?);"), Ok(2));
        assert_eq!(check_statement("SELECT 'a;b ? :x', \"col;\" FROM [we;ird] -- ; ?\n"), Ok(0));
        assert_eq!(check_statement("/* ; */ UPDATE t SET a = ? WHERE b = 'it''s'"), Ok(1));
        assert_eq!(check_statement("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS t (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, a$b TEXT)"), Ok(0));
        assert_eq!(check_statement("WITH x AS (SELECT ?) SELECT * FROM x"), Ok(1));
        let trigger = "CREATE TRIGGER tr AFTER INSERT ON t BEGIN UPDATE t SET a = CASE WHEN 1 THEN 2 END; DELETE FROM u; END;";
        assert_eq!(check_statement(trigger), Ok(0));
        assert_eq!(check_statement("create temp trigger tr after insert on t begin select 1; end"), Ok(0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn refuses_several_statements_named_parameters_and_control_keywords() {
        let e = |s: &str| check_statement(s).unwrap_err();
        assert!(e("SELECT 1; SELECT 2").contains("only one statement"));
        assert!(e("SELECT 1;;").contains("only one statement"));
        assert!(e("SELECT 1; -- x\n DROP TABLE t").contains("only one statement"));
        assert!(e("CREATE TRIGGER tr AFTER INSERT ON t BEGIN SELECT 1; END; DROP TABLE t").contains("only one statement"));
        assert!(e("SELECT 1; END; DROP TABLE t").contains("only one statement"), "only triggers span `;`");
        assert!(e("SELECT :id").contains("named parameters"));
        assert!(e("SELECT @id").contains("named parameters"));
        assert!(e("SELECT $id").contains("named parameters"));
        assert!(e("SELECT ?1").contains("numbered parameters"));
        for kw in ["ATTACH 'x' AS y", "detach y", "PRAGMA writable_schema = 1", "VACUUM INTO '/tmp/x'", "BEGIN", "COMMIT", "end", "ROLLBACK", "SAVEPOINT a", "RELEASE a", "EXPLAIN PRAGMA x", "explain query plan attach 'f' as g"] {
            assert!(e(kw).contains("not allowed"), "{kw}");
        }
        assert!(e("").contains("no statement"));
        assert!(e("  -- only a comment").contains("no statement"));
        assert!(e("SELECT 'open").contains("unterminated string"));
        assert!(e("SELECT /* open").contains("unterminated comment"));
        assert!(e("SELECT \"open").contains("unterminated"));
        assert!(e("SELECT 1\0").contains("NUL"));
        assert!(e(&format!("SELECT '{}'", "a".repeat(MAX_SQL_BYTES))).contains("longer than"));
    }
}
```

#[test]
fn records_and_enums_are_passed_as_arguments() {
    let r = runner(
        "type P = { x: Int, tags: List[Str] }\ntype E = A | B(P, Option[Int])\n\
         fn echo(e: E) -> E = e\nfn sum(ps: List[P]) -> Int = fold(ps, 0, add)\nfn add(acc: Int, p: P) -> Int = acc + p.x\n\
         fn path(rq: HttpRequest) -> Str = rq.method ++ \" \" ++ rq.path ++ \" \" ++ join(map(rq.headers, hname), \",\")\nfn hname(h: HttpHeader) -> Str = h.name\n\
         fn same(a: Result[Str, Int], b: Result[Str, Int]) -> Bool = a == b",
    );
    let p = |x: i64| Value::Record("P".into(), vec![("x".into(), Value::Int(x)), ("tags".into(), Value::List(vec![Value::Str("t".into())]))]);
    let b = Value::Variant("B".into(), vec![p(1), Value::Variant("Some".into(), vec![Value::Int(7)])]);
    assert_eq!(r.call("echo", std::slice::from_ref(&b)), Ok(b));
    assert_eq!(r.call("echo", &[Value::Variant("A".into(), vec![])]), Ok(Value::Variant("A".into(), vec![])));
    assert_eq!(r.call("sum", &[Value::List(vec![p(2), p(3)])]), Ok(Value::Int(5)));
    let h = |n: &str| Value::Record("HttpHeader".into(), vec![("name".into(), Value::Str(n.into())), ("value".into(), Value::Str("v".into()))]);
    let rq = Value::Record("HttpRequest".into(), vec![
        ("method".into(), Value::Str("GET".into())),
        ("path".into(), Value::Str("/todos".into())),
        ("query".into(), Value::Str(String::new())),
        ("headers".into(), Value::List(vec![h("host"), h("accept")])),
        ("body".into(), Value::Str(String::new())),
    ]);
    assert_eq!(r.call("path", &[rq]), Ok(Value::Str("GET /todos host,accept".into())));
    let ok = Value::Variant("Ok".into(), vec![Value::Str("a".into())]);
    assert_eq!(r.call("same", &[ok.clone(), ok]), Ok(Value::Bool(true)));
    let e = r.call("echo", &[Value::Variant("C".into(), vec![])]).unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!((e.kind, e.message.as_str()), ("bad_args", "argument 0 (`e`): expected E, got \"C\""));
    let e = r.call("sum", &[Value::List(vec![Value::Record("P".into(), vec![("x".into(), Value::Int(1))])])]).unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(e.kind, "bad_args");
}

#[test]
fn host_buffers_count_against_the_memory_limit() {
    let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("qal-run-hostmem-{}", std::process::id()));
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
    std::fs::write(root.join("big.txt"), vec![b'x'; 600 << 10]).unwrap();
    // 600 KiB of kept output plus a 600 KiB file exceed a 1 MiB limit together, not alone.
    let src = "fn main(args: List[Str]) -> Int !fs !io = { emit(15, slice(join(map(range(0, 4096), ten), \"\"), 0, 40959)); match read_file(match get(args, 0) { Some(p) => p, None => \"\" }) { Ok(s) => len(s), Err(_) => 0 - 1 } }\n\
               fn emit(n: Int, line: Str) -> Unit !io = if n == 0 then () else { print(line); emit(n - 1, line) }\n\
               fn ten(i: Int) -> Str = \"0123456789\"";
    let path = root.join("big.txt").to_string_lossy().into_owned();
    let r = main_runner(src, &format!("io,fs:{}", root.display())).with_memory_limit(4 << 20);
    assert_eq!(r.run_main(std::slice::from_ref(&path)).result, Ok(Value::Int(600 << 10)));
    let r = main_runner(src, &format!("io,fs:{}", root.display())).with_memory_limit(1 << 20).with_stdout_limit(usize::MAX);
    let e = r.run_main(&[path]).result.unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(e.kind, "out_of_memory");
    assert_eq!(serde_json::to_value(&e.values).unwrap()["host"], json!(true), "{e:?}");
    assert!(e.hints.iter().any(|h| h.contains("host's buffers")), "{:?}", e.hints);
}

#[test]
fn fs_writes_have_a_budget() {
    let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("qal-run-wbudget-{}", std::process::id()));
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
    let src = "fn main(args: List[Str]) -> Int !fs = go(0, match get(args, 0) { Some(d) => d, None => \".\" })\n\
               fn go(i: Int, d: Str) -> Int !fs = if i == 3 then 0 else match write_file(d ++ \"/f\" ++ from_int(i), \"0123456789\") { Ok(_) => go(i + 1, d), Err(_) => 9 }";
    let dir = root.to_string_lossy().into_owned();
    let run = |limits: Limits| main_runner(src, &format!("fs:{dir}")).with_limits(limits).run_main(std::slice::from_ref(&dir)).result;
    assert_eq!(run(Limits::default()), Ok(Value::Int(0)));
    let e = run(Limits { fs_writes: 2, ..Limits::default() }).unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!((e.kind, e.code), ("budget_exceeded", "E0522"));
    assert_eq!(serde_json::to_value(&e.values).unwrap(), json!({"budget": 2, "used": 3, "kind": "fs_writes"}));
    let e = run(Limits { fs_write_bytes: 25, ..Limits::default() }).unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(serde_json::to_value(&e.values).unwrap(), json!({"budget": 25, "used": 30, "kind": "fs_bytes"}));
    assert!(e.message.contains("at most 25 bytes"), "{}", e.message);
}

Create `qal/crates/qal-run/src/fs.rs`:

```rust
//! File access for the `fs` effect through verified directory descriptors (Phase 3 spec §5.3).
//!
//! Every granted directory is opened once, when the permission is parsed, as a descriptor
//! (`O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW | O_CLOEXEC`). A guest path is resolved **lexically** against the
//! granted directories (`.` and `..` are applied to the text; a path that leaves every granted
//! directory, a NUL byte, or a hidden component under `no_hidden` is refused uniformly) and then
//! opened component by component with `openat(dirfd, component, O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW)`, the
//! last one with `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK`. A symbolic link anywhere below the granted directory
//! makes `openat` fail, so nothing outside can be reached or created, whatever another process
//! swaps in between two steps; FIFOs and devices never block and are refused after `fstat`.

use std::path::{Component, Path, PathBuf};
use std::sync::Arc;

/// A granted directory: its canonical path, the absolute path it was granted as (both are accepted
/// in guest paths), and the descriptor every access starts from.
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct FsRoot {
    pub path: PathBuf,
    alias: PathBuf,
    #[cfg(unix)]
    fd: Arc<std::os::fd::OwnedFd>,
    #[cfg(not(unix))]
    _fd: Arc<()>,
}

impl std::fmt::Debug for FsRoot {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "FsRoot({})", self.path.display())
    }
}

impl PartialEq for FsRoot {
    fn eq(&self, other: &FsRoot) -> bool {
        self.path == other.path
    }
}

/// Lexically normalizes an absolute path (`.` dropped, `..` removes the previous component).
fn normalize(p: &Path) -> Option<PathBuf> {
    let mut out = PathBuf::new();
    for c in p.components() {
        match c {
            Component::Prefix(_) | Component::RootDir => out.push(c.as_os_str()),
            Component::CurDir => {}
            Component::ParentDir => {
                if !out.pop() {
                    return None;
                }
            }
            Component::Normal(n) => out.push(n),
        }
    }
    Some(out)
}

impl FsRoot {
    /// Opens `dir` (relative paths against the working directory) as a granted directory.
    pub fn open(dir: &Path) -> Result<FsRoot, String> {
        let shown = dir.display();
        let canonical = dir.canonicalize().map_err(|e| format!("fs:{shown}: {e}"))?;
        if !canonical.is_dir() {
            return Err(format!("fs:{shown}: not a directory"));
        }
        let alias = std::path::absolute(dir).ok().and_then(|a| normalize(&a)).unwrap_or_else(|| canonical.clone());
        #[cfg(unix)]
        {
            use rustix::fs::{Mode, OFlags};
            let fd = rustix::fs::open(&canonical, OFlags::RDONLY | OFlags::DIRECTORY | OFlags::NOFOLLOW | OFlags::CLOEXEC, Mode::empty()).map_err(|e| format!("fs:{shown}: {}", std::io::Error::from(e)))?;
            Ok(FsRoot { path: canonical, alias, fd: Arc::new(fd) })
        }
        #[cfg(not(unix))]
        {
            let _ = alias;
            Err(format!("fs:{shown}: the `fs` effect is not supported on this platform"))
        }
    }

    /// The components of `abs` below this root, if it lies inside (by its canonical path or the
    /// path it was granted as).
    fn below<'p>(&self, abs: &'p Path) -> Option<&'p Path> {
        abs.strip_prefix(&self.path).ok().or_else(|| abs.strip_prefix(&self.alias).ok())
    }
}

/// How a file is opened.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub enum Access {
    Read,
    /// Created if missing (mode 0o644); truncated only after it is known to be a regular file.
    Write,
}

/// Uniform refusal: identical for paths that exist and paths that do not.
pub fn denied(path: &str) -> String {
    format!("permission denied: `{path}` is outside the allowed directories")
}

/// Resolves the guest `path` against `base` (for relative paths; absolute paths stay) and finds
/// the granted root it lies in, with the components below that root.
pub fn locate<'r>(roots: &'r [FsRoot], base: &Path, path: &str, no_hidden: bool) -> Result<(&'r FsRoot, Vec<String>), String> {
    if path.contains('\0') || path.is_empty() {
        return Err(denied(path));
    }
    let joined = base.join(path);
    let abs = normalize(&joined).ok_or_else(|| denied(path))?;
    // The innermost root wins (a granted directory inside another one).
    let mut best: Option<(&FsRoot, Vec<String>)> = None;
    for r in roots {
        let Some(rel) = r.below(&abs) else { continue };
        let comps: Option<Vec<String>> = rel.components().map(|c| c.as_os_str().to_str().map(str::to_string)).collect();
        let Some(comps) = comps else { continue };
        if best.as_ref().is_none_or(|(_, b)| comps.len() < b.len()) {
            best = Some((r, comps));
        }
    }
    let (root, comps) = best.ok_or_else(|| denied(path))?;
    if no_hidden && comps.iter().any(|c| c.starts_with('.')) {
        return Err(denied(path));
    }
    Ok((root, comps))
}

/// Opens the guest `path` inside the granted `roots` without following any symbolic link below
/// the root and without blocking; the result is a regular file (for `Write`: not yet truncated).
#[cfg(unix)]
pub fn open(roots: &[FsRoot], base: &Path, path: &str, no_hidden: bool, access: Access) -> Result<std::fs::File, String> {
    use rustix::fs::{FileType, Mode, OFlags};
    use std::os::fd::{AsFd, OwnedFd};
    let (root, comps) = locate(roots, base, path, no_hidden)?;
    let Some((last, dirs)) = comps.split_last() else {
        return Err(format!("{path}: not a regular file"));
    };
    let io = |e: rustix::io::Errno| -> String {
        // A symbolic link on the way (ELOOP from O_NOFOLLOW, ENOTDIR from O_DIRECTORY on a link)
        // is refused like a path outside.
        if e == rustix::io::Errno::LOOP || e == rustix::io::Errno::NOTDIR || e == rustix::io::Errno::MLINK {
            denied(path)
        } else {
            format!("{path}: {}", std::io::Error::from(e))
        }
    };
    let dir_flags = OFlags::RDONLY | OFlags::DIRECTORY | OFlags::NOFOLLOW | OFlags::CLOEXEC;
    let mut current: Option<OwnedFd> = None;
    for d in dirs {
        let next = match &current {
            None => rustix::fs::openat(root.fd.as_fd(), d.as_str(), dir_flags, Mode::empty()),
            Some(fd) => rustix::fs::openat(fd.as_fd(), d.as_str(), dir_flags, Mode::empty()),
        }
        .map_err(io)?;
        current = Some(next);
    }
    let flags = OFlags::NOFOLLOW | OFlags::NONBLOCK | OFlags::CLOEXEC
        | match access {
            Access::Read => OFlags::RDONLY,
            Access::Write => OFlags::WRONLY | OFlags::CREATE,
        };
    let mode = Mode::from_raw_mode(0o644);
    let fd = match &current {
        None => rustix::fs::openat(root.fd.as_fd(), last.as_str(), flags, mode),
        Some(dir) => rustix::fs::openat(dir.as_fd(), last.as_str(), flags, mode),
    }
    .map_err(io)?;
    let st = rustix::fs::fstat(&fd).map_err(io)?;
    if FileType::from_raw_mode(st.st_mode) != FileType::RegularFile {
        return Err(format!("{path}: not a regular file"));
    }
    if access == Access::Write {
        rustix::fs::ftruncate(&fd, 0).map_err(io)?;
    }
    Ok(std::fs::File::from(fd))
}

#[cfg(not(unix))]
pub fn open(_: &[FsRoot], _: &Path, path: &str, _: bool, _: Access) -> Result<std::fs::File, String> {
    Err(format!("{path}: the `fs` effect is not supported on this platform"))
}

#[cfg(all(test, unix))]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use std::io::{Read, Write};

    fn dir(name: &str) -> PathBuf {
        let d = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("qal-fs-{name}-{}", std::process::id()));
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&d);
        std::fs::create_dir_all(d.join("data/sub")).unwrap();
        d
    }

    fn read(roots: &[FsRoot], base: &Path, p: &str) -> Result<String, String> {
        let mut s = String::new();
        open(roots, base, p, false, Access::Read)?.read_to_string(&mut s).unwrap();
        Ok(s)
    }

    #[test]
    fn paths_resolve_lexically_inside_the_granted_directories() {
        let d = dir("lex");
        std::fs::write(d.join("data/sub/a.txt"), "A").unwrap();
        std::fs::write(d.join("secret.txt"), "S").unwrap();
        let roots = vec![FsRoot::open(&d.join("data")).unwrap()];
        let base = d.join("data");
        assert_eq!(read(&roots, &base, "sub/a.txt").unwrap(), "A");
        assert_eq!(read(&roots, &base, "./sub/../sub/a.txt").unwrap(), "A");
        assert_eq!(read(&roots, &base, &d.join("data/sub/a.txt").to_string_lossy()).unwrap(), "A", "absolute path as granted");
        assert_eq!(read(&roots, &base, &d.canonicalize().unwrap().join("data/sub/a.txt").to_string_lossy()).unwrap(), "A", "canonical path");
        for outside in ["../secret.txt", "sub/../../secret.txt", "/etc/hosts", "/nonexistent/../etc/hosts", "", "a\0b"] {
            assert_eq!(read(&roots, &base, outside).unwrap_err(), denied(outside), "{outside:?}");
        }
        assert!(read(&roots, &base, "sub/missing.txt").unwrap_err().starts_with("sub/missing.txt: No such file"));
        assert_eq!(read(&roots, &base, "sub").unwrap_err(), "sub: not a regular file");
        assert_eq!(read(&roots, &base, ".").unwrap_err(), ".: not a regular file");
        assert_eq!(locate(&roots, &base, "sub/.env", true).unwrap_err(), denied("sub/.env"), "hidden components are refused under no_hidden");
        assert_eq!(locate(&roots, &base, "sub/.env", false).unwrap().1, vec!["sub".to_string(), ".env".to_string()]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn symlinks_anywhere_below_the_root_are_refused() {
        let d = dir("links");
        std::fs::write(d.join("secret.txt"), "S").unwrap();
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&d, d.join("data/up")).unwrap();
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(d.join("secret.txt"), d.join("data/file-link")).unwrap();
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(d.join("nowhere.txt"), d.join("data/dangling")).unwrap();
        let roots = vec![FsRoot::open(&d.join("data")).unwrap()];
        let base = d.join("data");
        for p in ["up/secret.txt", "file-link", "dangling"] {
            assert_eq!(read(&roots, &base, p).unwrap_err(), denied(p), "{p}");
            assert_eq!(open(&roots, &base, p, false, Access::Write).unwrap_err(), denied(p), "{p}");
        }
        assert!(!d.join("nowhere.txt").exists(), "writing through a dangling link creates nothing");
        assert_eq!(std::fs::read_to_string(d.join("secret.txt")).unwrap(), "S");
    }

    #[test]
    fn swapping_a_directory_for_a_symlink_creates_nothing_outside() {
        // The race of Phase 1 (`check_path`, then a swap, then `open`) cannot happen: each component
        // is opened relative to the verified descriptor of its parent.
        let d = dir("swap");
        let outside = d.join("outside");
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&outside).unwrap();
        let roots = vec![FsRoot::open(&d.join("data")).unwrap()];
        let base = d.join("data");
        // Step 1: the path is fine.
        open(&roots, &base, "sub/new.txt", false, Access::Write).unwrap().write_all(b"x").unwrap();
        // Step 2: `sub` becomes a link to a directory outside.
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(d.join("data/sub")).unwrap();
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&outside, d.join("data/sub")).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(open(&roots, &base, "sub/new2.txt", false, Access::Write).unwrap_err(), denied("sub/new2.txt"));
        assert_eq!(std::fs::read_dir(&outside).unwrap().count(), 0, "no file was created outside");
        // The granted directory itself is held open: replacing it does not redirect the descriptor.
        std::fs::rename(d.join("data"), d.join("moved")).unwrap();
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&outside, d.join("data")).unwrap();
        open(&roots, &base, "kept.txt", false, Access::Write).unwrap();
        assert!(d.join("moved/kept.txt").exists() && !outside.join("kept.txt").exists());
    }

    #[test]
    fn writing_truncates_only_regular_files() {
        let d = dir("trunc");
        std::fs::write(d.join("data/a.txt"), "long old content").unwrap();
        let roots = vec![FsRoot::open(&d.join("data")).unwrap()];
        let mut f = open(&roots, &d.join("data"), "a.txt", false, Access::Write).unwrap();
        f.write_all(b"new").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(std::fs::read_to_string(d.join("data/a.txt")).unwrap(), "new");
        assert!(std::process::Command::new("mkfifo").arg(d.join("data/pipe")).status().unwrap().success());
        let t = std::time::Instant::now();
        assert!(open(&roots, &d.join("data"), "pipe", false, Access::Read).unwrap_err().ends_with("not a regular file"));
        assert!(open(&roots, &d.join("data"), "pipe", false, Access::Write).unwrap_err().starts_with("pipe: "));
        assert!(t.elapsed() < std::time::Duration::from_secs(2));
        assert!(FsRoot::open(&d.join("data/a.txt")).unwrap_err().contains("not a directory"));
        assert!(FsRoot::open(&d.join("nope")).is_err());
    }
}

Create `qal/crates/qal-run/src/guest.rs`:

```rust
//! Writing host values into guest memory: any QAL value (Int, Float, Bool, Unit, Str, List,
//! records, enums, Option, Result) in the layout of `qal_wasm`, allocated with the module's own
//! `qal_alloc` (so the memory limit and `out_of_memory` apply exactly as for guest allocations).

use crate::host::HostState;
use crate::value::Value;
use qal_core::ast::Type;
use qal_core::tast::TProgram;
use wasmtime::{AsContextMut, Memory, TypedFunc};

/// Values nested deeper than this are refused (host values are shallow; this guards the stack).
const MAX_DEPTH: usize = 200;

/// The guest's memory and allocator.
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Guest {
    pub mem: Memory,
    alloc: TypedFunc<i64, i32>,
}

/// Why a value could not be written: the guest trapped (out of memory: the failure is recorded in
/// the host state) or the value does not have the type.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub enum EncodeError {
    Trap(wasmtime::Error),
    Mismatch(String),
}

impl From<wasmtime::Error> for EncodeError {
    fn from(e: wasmtime::Error) -> EncodeError {
        EncodeError::Trap(e)
    }
}

impl Guest {
    pub fn new(mem: Memory, alloc: TypedFunc<i64, i32>) -> Guest {
        Guest { mem, alloc }
    }

    fn block(&self, cx: &mut impl AsContextMut<Data = HostState>, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<i32, EncodeError> {
        let p = self.alloc.call(&mut *cx, bytes.len() as i64)?;
        self.mem.write(&mut *cx, p as usize, bytes).map_err(|e| EncodeError::Trap(wasmtime::Error::msg(e.to_string())))?;
        Ok(p)
    }

    /// Writes `v` (of type `ty`) and returns its slot: the scalar itself or the pointer.
    pub fn encode(&self, cx: &mut impl AsContextMut<Data = HostState>, tp: &TProgram, ty: &Type, v: &Value) -> Result<i64, EncodeError> {
        self.encode_at(cx, tp, ty, v, 0)
    }

    fn encode_at(&self, cx: &mut impl AsContextMut<Data = HostState>, tp: &TProgram, ty: &Type, v: &Value, depth: usize) -> Result<i64, EncodeError> {
        let mismatch = || EncodeError::Mismatch(format!("expected {ty}, got {}", serde_json::to_string(v).unwrap_or_default().chars().take(60).collect::<String>()));
        if depth > MAX_DEPTH {
            return Err(EncodeError::Mismatch("value nested too deeply".into()));
        }
        Ok(match (ty, v) {
            (Type::Int, Value::Int(n)) => *n,
            (Type::Float, Value::Float(x)) => x.to_bits() as i64,
            (Type::Bool, Value::Bool(b)) => *b as i64,
            (Type::Unit, Value::Unit) => 0,
            (Type::Str, Value::Str(s)) => {
                let mut b = Vec::with_capacity(4 + s.len());
                b.extend((s.len() as u32).to_le_bytes());
                b.extend(s.as_bytes());
                self.block(cx, &b)? as i64
            }
            (Type::List(elem), Value::List(xs)) => {
                let mut b = Vec::with_capacity(8 + 8 * xs.len());
                b.extend((xs.len() as u32).to_le_bytes());
                b.extend([0; 4]);
                for x in xs {
                    b.extend(self.encode_at(cx, tp, elem, x, depth + 1)?.to_le_bytes());
                }
                self.block(cx, &b)? as i64
            }
            (Type::Named(_), Value::Record(_, fields)) if tp.is_record(ty) => {
                let decl = tp.record_fields(ty);
                if fields.len() != decl.len() {
                    return Err(mismatch());
                }
                let mut b = Vec::with_capacity(8 * decl.len());
                for (name, ft) in &decl {
                    let x = fields.iter().find(|(n, _)| n == name).map(|(_, x)| x).ok_or_else(mismatch)?;
                    b.extend(self.encode_at(cx, tp, ft, x, depth + 1)?.to_le_bytes());
                }
                // A record without fields still needs a distinct, valid address.
                if b.is_empty() {
                    b.extend([0; 8]);
                }
                self.block(cx, &b)? as i64
            }
            (Type::Named(_) | Type::Option(_) | Type::Result(..), Value::Variant(name, payload)) => {
                let tag = tp.variant_names(ty).iter().position(|n| n == name).ok_or_else(mismatch)? as u32;
                let fields = tp.variant_fields(ty, tag);
                if fields.len() != payload.len() {
                    return Err(mismatch());
                }
                let mut b = Vec::with_capacity(8 + 8 * fields.len());
                b.extend(tag.to_le_bytes());
                b.extend([0; 4]);
                for (ft, x) in fields.iter().zip(payload) {
                    b.extend(self.encode_at(cx, tp, ft, x, depth + 1)?.to_le_bytes());
                }
                self.block(cx, &b)? as i64
            }
            _ => return Err(mismatch()),
        })
    }
}

In `qal/crates/qal-run/src/host.rs`, replace:

```rust
    }

    fn inside(&self, p: &Path) -> bool {
        self.fs.iter().any(|root| p.strip_prefix(root).is_ok_and(|rel| !(self.no_hidden && hidden(rel))))
    }

    /// A guest path as the host sees it: relative paths are taken from `base` if set.
    fn resolve(&self, path: &str) -> PathBuf {
        match &self.base {
            Some(base) => base.join(path),
            None => PathBuf::from(path),
        }
    }

    /// Uniform refusal: identical for paths that exist and paths that do not, so file existence
    /// outside the granted directories cannot be probed.
    fn denied(path: &str) -> String {
        format!("permission denied: `{path}` is outside the allowed directories")
    }

    /// A path that could not be resolved: the real error is only shown if the nearest existing
    /// ancestor lies inside a granted directory, otherwise it is the uniform refusal.
    fn unresolved(&self, p: &Path, path: &str, err: std::io::Error) -> String {
        for anc in p.ancestors().skip(1) {
            let anc = if anc.as_os_str().is_empty() { Path::new(".") } else { anc };
            if let Ok(c) = anc.canonicalize() {
                return if self.inside(&c) { format!("{path}: {err}") } else { Self::denied(path) };
            }
        }
        Self::denied(path)
    }

    /// Resolves `path` (relative to the working directory) and checks it lies inside a granted directory.
    /// For writing, the file itself may not exist yet, but its directory must.
    pub fn check_path(&self, path: &str, for_write: bool) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
        let p = &self.resolve(path);
        let resolved = match p.canonicalize() {
            Ok(c) => c,
            Err(e) if !for_write => return Err(self.unresolved(p, path, e)),
            Err(e) => {
                let parent = match p.parent() {
                    Some(d) if !d.as_os_str().is_empty() => d,
                    _ => Path::new("."),
                };
                let Some(name) = p.file_name() else { return Err(self.unresolved(p, path, e)) };
                let full = match parent.canonicalize() {
                    Ok(d) => d.join(name),
                    Err(e) => return Err(self.unresolved(p, path, e)),
                };
                if self.inside(&full) && std::fs::symlink_metadata(p).is_ok() {
                    // A dangling symlink "does not exist" but writing would follow it, possibly outside.
                    return Err(format!("permission denied: `{path}` is a dangling symbolic link"));
                }
                full
            }
        };
        if self.inside(&resolved) {
            Ok(resolved)
        } else {
            Err(Self::denied(path))
        }
    }

    /// After opening, re-resolves `resolved` and requires that it is still inside a granted directory
    /// and is the very file that was opened (guards against links swapped in after `check_path`).
    pub fn verify_opened(&self, file: &File, resolved: &Path) -> Result<(), String> {
        let changed = || format!("permission denied: `{}` changed while being opened", resolved.display());
        let now = resolved.canonicalize().map_err(|_| changed())?;
        if !self.inside(&now) {
            return Err(changed());
        }
        let (a, b) = (file.metadata().map_err(|_| changed())?, std::fs::metadata(&now).map_err(|_| changed())?);
        if same_file(&a, &b) {
            Ok(())
        } else {
            Err(changed())
        }
    }

    /// Checks, opens without following a final symlink and without blocking, and verifies that the
    /// opened file is a regular file (a FIFO or device would block the host or never end).
    fn open(&self, path: &str, for_write: bool) -> Result<File, String> {
        let full = self.check_path(path, for_write)?;
        let file = open_nofollow(&full, for_write).map_err(|e| format!("{path}: {e}"))?;
        self.verify_opened(&file, &full)?;
        if !file.metadata().map(|m| m.is_file()).unwrap_or(false) {
            return Err(format!("{path}: not a regular file"));
        }
        Ok(file)
    }
}
