}

/// Runs requests through the service pipeline of `qal serve` without TCP: same limits, same
/// transaction rule, same response checks. `init` runs first; all requests share one database
/// (`db:mem`: a fresh one for this call).
pub fn request(ctx: &Ctx, source: &Source, mut perms: Permissions, handler: &str, init: Option<&str>, requests: &[RequestSpec]) -> Reply {
    if requests.len() > MAX_REQUESTS {
        let d = Diagnostic::new("E0701", "bad_arguments", "", "", format!("{} requests; at most {MAX_REQUESTS} per call", requests.len()));
        return Reply::diagnostics(vec![d]);
    }
    let loaded = match ctx.load(source) {
        Ok(l) => l,
        Err(d) => return Reply::diagnostics(d),
    };
    if let (Some(_), None, StoreAt::Discover(dir)) = (&perms.db, &perms.db_dir, &ctx.store) {
        if let Some(project) = qal_store::project_of(dir) {
            perms = perms.with_db_dir(project.join(qal_store::STORE_DIR).join("db"));
        }
    }
    let cfg = qal_serve::ServiceConfig { entry: handler.to_string(), init: init.map(str::to_string), slots: 2, max_out: ctx.max_stdout.unwrap_or(64 << 10), ..qal_serve::ServiceConfig::default() };
    let limits = qal_serve::HttpLimits::default();
    // Guest code needs a large native stack (the CLI's main thread has a small one).
    let run = || -> Result<Vec<RequestResult>, RunError> {
        let service = qal_serve::Service::new(&loaded.typed, perms, cfg)?;
        let mut worker = service.worker()?;
        service.init(&mut worker)?;
        Ok(requests
            .iter()
            .map(|spec| {
                let id = service.next_id();
                let req = &spec.request;
                if req.body.len() > limits.max_body {
                    let e = RunError::new("request_rejected", handler, format!("413 payload_too_large: the body has {} bytes, more than the limit of {}", req.body.len(), limits.max_body));
                    let resp = qal_serve::http::Rejection { status: 413, kind: "payload_too_large", message: e.message.clone() }.response(id);
                    return result_of(resp, "none", None, Some(e));
                }
                let mut handler_req = req.clone();
                if req.method == "HEAD" {
                    handler_req.method = "GET".into();
                }
                let h = service.handle(&mut worker, &handler_req, id, 0);
                let mut resp = h.response;
                if req.method == "HEAD" {
                    resp.body = String::new();
                }
                result_of(resp, h.log.db, h.log.out, h.log.error)
            })
            .collect())
    };
    let outcome = std::thread::scope(|s| match std::thread::Builder::new().name("qal-request".into()).stack_size(qal_run::GUEST_THREAD_STACK).spawn_scoped(s, run) {
        Ok(t) => t.join().unwrap_or_else(|_| Err(RunError::new("internal", handler, "the runtime panicked while running the requests"))),
        Err(e) => Err(RunError::new("trap", handler, format!("cannot start the execution thread: {e}"))),
    });
    match outcome {
        Ok(responses) => Reply::new(Output { responses: Some(responses), ..Output::default() }, 0),
        Err(e) => Reply::error(e),
    }
}

fn result_of(resp: qal_serve::Response, db: &'static str, stdout: Option<String>, error: Option<RunError>) -> RequestResult {
    let is_json = resp.header("content-type").is_some_and(|t| t.trim_start().to_ascii_lowercase().starts_with("application/json"));
    let json = if is_json { serde_json::from_str(&resp.body).ok() } else { None };
    let headers = resp.headers.iter().map(|(n, v)| Value::Record("HttpHeader".into(), vec![("name".into(), Value::Str(n.clone())), ("value".into(), Value::Str(v.clone()))])).collect();
    RequestResult { status: resp.status, headers, body: resp.body, json, db, stdout, error: error.map(capped) }
```

In `qal/crates/qal-cli/src/reference.md`, replace:

```markdown
Performance: push on a list or ++ on a string in a loop is quadratic (about 8 000 elements at most): build lists with split/map/filter/range. len and slice on Str are O(n): iterate with chars.

Store rules: defining an existing name replaces it. Same signature: everything using it is relinked and re-checked automatically. Changed signature: rejected (`signature_change`, lists `dependents`) unless the same call also redefines those dependents. A name keeps its kind (fn, test, type). References bind to names: qal_rename changes no hash and users follow the new name. Two names can hold the identical definition, but one definition cannot refer to both (E0404 `alias_reference`): use one name.
Workflow: qal_define -> qal_test -> qal_run (entry point; grant effects with allow: ["io"], "fs:<dir>" grants read AND write of all non-hidden files under that project dir, e.g. Cargo.toml: grant the narrowest dir) or qal_call (any fn, JSON args). Read the codebase with qal_query and qal_view.
```

with:

```markdown
Performance: push on a list or ++ on a string in a loop is quadratic (about 8 000 elements at most): build lists with split/map/filter/range. len and slice on Str are O(n): iterate with chars.

Services (prelude types exist in every program; their names and variants are reserved):
  HttpHeader = { name: Str, value: Str }  HttpRequest = { method: Str, path: Str, query: Str, headers: List[HttpHeader], body: Str }
  HttpResponse = { status: Int, headers: List[HttpHeader], body: Str }  HttpCall = { method: Str, url: Str, headers: List[HttpHeader], body: Str }
  A service handler is `fn handle(rq: HttpRequest) -> HttpResponse` (any effects; header names arrive lowercase); test it with qal_request.
  db_exec(sql, params) -> Result[Int, DbError] !db (changed rows)  db_query(sql, params) -> Result[DbRows, DbError] !db
    sql must be a string literal with `?` placeholders, one statement, no PRAGMA/ATTACH/BEGIN/COMMIT (E0241, E0242); values go in params: [SqlInt(id), SqlText(t)]
    SqlValue = SqlNull | SqlInt(Int) | SqlFloat(Float) | SqlText(Str)  DbRows = { columns: List[Str], rows: List[List[SqlValue]] }
    DbError = DbConstraint(Str) | DbSql(Str) | DbBusy | DbFull | DbTooLarge. One transaction per execution, committed only for status 100-399 (qal_call: no error, qal_run: exit 0).
  http(call) -> Result[HttpResponse, NetError] !net  NetError = NetDenied(Str) | NetInvalid(Str) | NetConnect(Str) | NetTimeout | NetTooLarge; redirects are not followed.
  Grant "db:mem" (fresh in-memory database), "db" or "db:<name>" (.qal/db/<name>.sqlite), "net:<host>[:port]". Library: qal_query name "std.".
Store rules: defining an existing name replaces it. Same signature: everything using it is relinked and re-checked automatically. Changed signature: rejected (`signature_change`, lists `dependents`) unless the same call also redefines those dependents. A name keeps its kind (fn, test, type). References bind to names: qal_rename changes no hash and users follow the new name. Two names can hold the identical definition, but one definition cannot refer to both (E0404 `alias_reference`): use one name.
Workflow: qal_define -> qal_test -> qal_run (entry point; grant effects with allow: ["io"], "fs:<dir>" grants read AND write of all non-hidden files under that project dir, e.g. Cargo.toml: grant the narrowest dir) or qal_call (any fn, JSON args). Read the codebase with qal_query and qal_view.

#[test]
fn packages_round_trip_between_projects() {
    let a = temp_dir().join("pkg-a");
    let b = temp_dir().join("pkg-b");
    for d in [&a, &b] {
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(d);
        std::fs::create_dir_all(d).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(qal_json_in(d, &["init"]).0, 0);
    }
    assert_eq!(qal_json_in(&a, &["define", &example("math.qal")]).0, 0);
    let (code, v) = qal_json_in(&a, &["export", "", "--out", "out/math.qalpkg"]);
    assert_eq!((code, v["exported"]["package"].as_str(), v["exported"]["defs"].as_u64()), (0, Some("math"), Some(8)));
    let file = a.join("out/math.qalpkg");
    let (code, v) = qal_json_in(&b, &["import", file.to_str().unwrap()]);
    assert_eq!((code, v["imported"]["defined"].as_array().map(|d| d.len())), (0, Some(8)), "{v}");
    let (_, qa) = qal_json_in(&a, &["query"]);
    let (_, qb) = qal_json_in(&b, &["query"]);
    assert_eq!(qa, qb, "identical names and hashes");

}

#[test]
fn packages_export_and_import_with_identical_hashes() {
    let mut s = store();
    ok(&mut s, "type P = { x: Int }\nfn lib_x(p: P) -> Int = p.x\nfn lib_two() -> Int = lib_x(P { x: 2 }) + helper()\nfn helper() -> Int = 0\nfn other() -> Int = 9\ntest lib_t = lib_two() == 2");
    let e = s.export("lib_", "lib").unwrap();
    assert_eq!((e.package.as_str(), e.defs), ("lib", 5), "the prefix plus dependencies P and helper");
    let lines: Vec<&str> = e.file.lines().collect();
    assert_eq!(lines[0], "# qal-pkg 1");
    assert_eq!(lines[1], format!("# package lib {}", e.hash));
    assert!(lines[2].starts_with("# def P ") && lines[2].ends_with(" test"), "{}", lines[2]);
    assert!(!e.file.contains("fn other"));
    let text = e.file.split("\n\n").skip(1).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n\n");
    assert!(text.find("type P").unwrap() < text.find("fn lib_x").unwrap() && text.find("fn helper").unwrap() < text.find("fn lib_two").unwrap(), "dependencies first");
    assert_eq!(s.export("lib_", "lib").unwrap().file, e.file, "deterministic");
    // Into a fresh store: same hashes, recorded origin, package hash in meta.
    let mut t = store();
    let i = t.import(&e.file).unwrap();
    assert_eq!((i.package.as_str(), i.hash.as_str(), i.defined.len()), ("lib", e.hash.as_str(), 5));
    for n in ["P", "lib_x", "lib_two", "helper", "lib_t"] {
        assert_eq!(hash(&mut t, n), hash(&mut s, n), "{n}");
    }
    assert_eq!(t.history("lib_x").unwrap()[0].agent, format!("qal-lib@{}", &e.hash[..12]));
    assert_eq!(t.meta("package:lib").unwrap(), Some(e.hash.clone()));
    assert_eq!(statuses(&t.import(&e.file).unwrap().defined)[0].1, "unchanged", "importing again changes nothing");
    // A changed file: wrong checksum, or right checksum but other hashes -> nothing is written.
    let tampered = e.file.replace("p.x", "p.x + 1");
    let d = t.import(&tampered).unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!((d[0].code, d[0].kind), ("E0243", "package_mismatch"));
    let body = tampered.split_once('\n').unwrap().1.split_once('\n').unwrap().1;
    let resealed = format!("# qal-pkg 1\n# package lib {}\n{body}", Hash::of(body.as_bytes()).to_hex());
    let mut u = store();
    let d = u.import(&resealed).unwrap_err();
    assert!(d[0].code == "E0243" && d[0].message.contains("was recorded with hash"), "{d:?}");
    assert_eq!(count(&u, "names"), 0, "rolled back");
    assert_eq!(u.import("not a package").unwrap_err()[0].code, "E0243");
    assert!(s.export("x", "Bad Name").is_err());
}

#[test]
fn an_empty_package_is_valid_and_old_stores_are_migrated() {
    let mut s = store();
    let e = s.export("std.", "std").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(e.file, format!("# qal-pkg 1\n# package std {}\n", Hash::of(b"").to_hex()));
    assert_eq!(s.import(&e.file).unwrap().defined.len(), 0);
    assert_eq!(s.meta("package:std").unwrap(), Some(Hash::of(b"").to_hex()));
    // A version-2 store (no `meta`) is upgraded when opened.
    let mut old = store();
    ok(&mut old, "fn f() -> Int = 1");
    let c = raw(&old);
    c.execute_batch("DROP TABLE meta; PRAGMA user_version = 2;").unwrap();
    drop(c);
    let path = old.path().to_path_buf();
    drop(old);
    let mut again = Store::open(&path).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(again.meta("x").unwrap(), None);
    assert_eq!(hashes(&mut again).len(), 1);
    let v: i64 = raw(&again).query_row("PRAGMA user_version", [], |r| r.get(0)).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(v, 3);
}

}

/// What `export` wrote.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize)]
pub struct Exported {
    pub package: String,
    pub hash: String,
    pub defs: usize,
    pub file: String,
}

/// Exports the names starting with `prefix` (and their dependencies) to the package file `out`;
/// the package is named `name`, by default after the file (`std/std.qalpkg` -> `std`).
pub fn export(ctx: &Ctx, prefix: &str, out: &Path, name: Option<&str>) -> Reply {
    let stem = out.file_stem().and_then(|s| s.to_str()).unwrap_or("");
    let package = name.unwrap_or(stem);
    let exported = match ctx.open().and_then(|mut s| s.export(prefix, package)) {
        Ok(e) => e,
        Err(d) => return Reply::diagnostics(d),
    };
    if let Err(e) = out.parent().filter(|p| !p.as_os_str().is_empty()).map_or(Ok(()), std::fs::create_dir_all).and_then(|_| write_atomic(out, exported.file.as_bytes())) {
        return Reply::diagnostics(vec![Diagnostic::store_error(format!("cannot write {}: {e}", out.display()))]);
    }
    let e = Exported { package: exported.package, hash: exported.hash, defs: exported.defs, file: out.display().to_string() };
    Reply::new(Output { exported: Some(e), ..Output::default() }, 0)
}

/// Imports a package file into the store (atomically; every hash is verified).
pub fn import(ctx: &Ctx, file: &str) -> Reply {
    match ctx.open_store(true).and_then(|mut s| s.import(file)) {
        Ok(i) => Reply::new(Output { imported: Some(i), ..Output::default() }, 0),
        Err(d) => Reply::diagnostics(d),
    }
```

Create `qal/crates/qal-store/src/package.rs`:

````rust
//! Packages: named definitions exported as text with their hashes and origin, and imported with
//! the guarantee that the same text gives the same hashes (Phase 3 spec §4.3).
//!
//! ```text
//! # qal-pkg 1
//! # package <name> <sha256 of everything after this line>
//! # def <name> <hash> <agent>          (one line per name, sorted by name)
//!
//! <canonical text of all definitions, dependencies first, separated by blank lines>
//! ```
//!
//! `agent` is who last added or replaced the definition (the recorded origin). Importing defines
//! the text in one atomic `define` and keeps it only if every name gets exactly the recorded hash.

use crate::define;
use crate::snapshot::Snapshot;
use crate::{sql, Defined};
use qal_core::ast::Program;
use qal_core::canon::Hash;
use qal_core::diag::Diagnostic;
use rusqlite::{params, Connection, OptionalExtension};
use serde::Serialize;
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

pub const FORMAT_LINE: &str = "# qal-pkg 1";

/// One `# def` line.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize)]
pub struct PackageDef {
    pub name: String,
    pub hash: String,
    pub agent: String,
}

/// A parsed, checksum-verified package.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Package {
    pub name: String,
    pub hash: String,
    pub defs: Vec<PackageDef>,
    /// The definitions' text.
    pub text: String,
}

/// The result of an export.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize)]
pub struct Exported {
    pub package: String,
    pub hash: String,
    pub defs: usize,
    #[serde(skip)]
    pub file: String,
}

/// The result of an import.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize)]
pub struct Imported {
    pub package: String,
    pub hash: String,
    pub defined: Vec<Defined>,
}

fn mismatch(message: impl Into<String>) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    vec![Diagnostic::new("E0243", "package_mismatch", "", "", message).hint("the package file was changed or damaged; export it again from its source project")]
}

/// A package name: lowercase letters, digits, `_` and `-`, starting with a letter.
pub fn valid_package_name(n: &str) -> bool {
    (1..=64).contains(&n.len()) && n.starts_with(|c: char| c.is_ascii_lowercase()) && n.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_lowercase() || c.is_ascii_digit() || c == '_' || c == '-')
}

/// Parses and verifies a package file (format, checksum, `# def` lines).
pub fn parse(file: &str) -> Result<Package, Vec<Diagnostic>> {
    let (first, rest) = file.split_once('\n').ok_or_else(|| mismatch("not a QAL package: the header is missing"))?;
    if first != FORMAT_LINE {
        return Err(mismatch(format!("not a QAL package of format 1: the first line is `{}`", first.chars().take(40).collect::<String>())));
    }
    let (pkg_line, body) = rest.split_once('\n').unwrap_or((rest, ""));
    let mut parts = pkg_line.split(' ');
    let (Some("#"), Some("package"), Some(name), Some(hash), None) = (parts.next(), parts.next(), parts.next(), parts.next(), parts.next()) else {
        return Err(mismatch("the second line must be `# package <name> <sha256>`"));
    };
    if Hash::of(body.as_bytes()).to_hex() != hash {
        return Err(mismatch(format!("the checksum of package `{name}` does not match its content")));
    }
    let mut defs = Vec::new();
    let mut lines = body.split_inclusive('\n');
    let mut offset = 0;
    for line in lines.by_ref() {
        let Some(def) = line.strip_prefix("# def ") else { break };
        offset += line.len();
        let def = def.trim_end_matches('\n');
        let mut p = def.splitn(3, ' ');
        let (Some(n), Some(h), Some(agent)) = (p.next(), p.next(), p.next()) else { return Err(mismatch(format!("malformed `# def` line `{def}`"))) };
        defs.push(PackageDef { name: n.into(), hash: h.into(), agent: agent.into() });
    }
    Ok(Package { name: name.into(), hash: hash.into(), defs, text: body[offset..].to_string() })
}

/// Builds the package file of `name` from the definitions in `text` and their `# def` lines.
fn assemble(name: &str, defs: &[PackageDef], text: &str) -> (String, String) {
    let mut body = String::new();
    for d in defs {
        body.push_str(&format!("# def {} {} {}\n", d.name, d.hash, d.agent));
    }
    if !text.is_empty() {
        body.push('\n');
        body.push_str(text);
    }
    let hash = Hash::of(body.as_bytes()).to_hex();
    (format!("{FORMAT_LINE}\n# package {name} {hash}\n{body}"), hash)
}

/// The names a package of `prefix` holds: the matching names and everything they depend on.
fn closure(snap: &Snapshot, prefix: &str) -> BTreeSet<String> {
    let mut out = BTreeSet::new();
    let mut todo: Vec<String> = snap.names.keys().filter(|n| n.starts_with(prefix)).cloned().collect();
    while let Some(n) = todo.pop() {
        if out.insert(n.clone()) {
            if let Some(e) = snap.names.get(&n) {
                todo.extend(e.refs.iter().cloned());
            }
        }
    }
    out
}

/// `names` with dependencies first; ties (and mutually recursive groups) by name.
fn dependency_order(snap: &Snapshot, names: &BTreeSet<String>) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut deps: BTreeMap<&str, BTreeSet<&str>> = names.iter().map(|n| (n.as_str(), snap.names.get(n).map(|e| e.refs.iter().map(String::as_str).filter(|r| names.contains(*r) && *r != n).collect()).unwrap_or_default())).collect();
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    while !deps.is_empty() {
        let ready: Vec<&str> = deps.iter().filter(|(_, ds)| ds.is_empty()).map(|(n, _)| *n).collect();
        // A cycle (mutual recursion): take its smallest name; define handles the group at once.
        let take = if ready.is_empty() { deps.keys().take(1).copied().collect() } else { ready };
        for n in take {
            deps.remove(n);
            for ds in deps.values_mut() {
                ds.remove(n);
            }
            out.push(n.to_string());
        }
    }
    out
}

/// Exports every name starting with `prefix` (and its dependencies) as package `package`.
pub fn export(conn: &mut Connection, prefix: &str, package: &str) -> Result<Exported, Vec<Diagnostic>> {
    if !valid_package_name(package) {
        return Err(vec![Diagnostic::new("E0701", "bad_arguments", "", "", format!("`{package}` is not a package name")).hint("package names are 1 to 64 characters `a-z`, `0-9`, `_`, `-`, starting with a letter")]);
    }
    let tx = conn.transaction().map_err(sql)?;
    let snap = Snapshot::load(&tx)?;
    let names = closure(&snap, prefix);
    let mut defs = Vec::new();
    for n in &names {
        let agent: Option<String> = tx
            .query_row("SELECT agent FROM history WHERE name = ?1 AND action IN ('added', 'replaced') ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1", params![n], |r| r.get(0))
            .optional()
            .map_err(sql)?;
        defs.push(PackageDef { name: n.clone(), hash: snap.names[n].hash.to_hex(), agent: agent.unwrap_or_else(|| "unknown".into()) });
    }
    let mut texts = Vec::new();
    for n in dependency_order(&snap, &names) {
        let d = snap.decode_name(&tx, &n)?;
        texts.push(qal_text::print_program(&Program { defs: vec![d] }).trim_end().to_string());
    }
    let text = if texts.is_empty() { String::new() } else { texts.join("\n\n") + "\n" };
    let (file, hash) = assemble(package, &defs, &text);
    Ok(Exported { package: package.into(), hash, defs: defs.len(), file })
}

/// Imports a package: one atomic `define` of its text (agent `qal-<package>@<hash prefix>`), kept
/// only if every name gets the recorded hash (`package_mismatch`, E0243, otherwise; nothing is
/// written). Records `package:<name>` in `meta`.
pub fn import(conn: &mut Connection, file: &str) -> Result<Imported, Vec<Diagnostic>> {
    let pkg = parse(file)?;
    if !valid_package_name(&pkg.name) {
        return Err(mismatch(format!("`{}` is not a package name", pkg.name)));
    }
    let agent = format!("qal-{}@{}", pkg.name, &pkg.hash[..12]);
    let expected: BTreeMap<&str, &str> = pkg.defs.iter().map(|d| (d.name.as_str(), d.hash.as_str())).collect();
    let verify = |defined: &[Defined], tx: &rusqlite::Transaction| -> Result<(), Vec<Diagnostic>> {
        for (name, hash) in &expected {
            let stored: Option<String> = tx.query_row("SELECT hash FROM names WHERE name = ?1", params![name], |r| r.get(0)).optional().map_err(sql)?;
            if stored.as_deref() != Some(*hash) {
                return Err(mismatch(format!("`{name}` was recorded with hash {} but defines as {}", &hash[..hash.len().min(12)], stored.as_deref().map_or("nothing", |s| &s[..s.len().min(12)]))));
            }
        }
        if let Some(extra) = defined.iter().find(|d| !expected.contains_key(d.name.as_str()) && d.status != "relinked") {
            return Err(mismatch(format!("`{}` is defined by the package text but not listed in it", extra.name)));
        }
        tx.execute("INSERT INTO meta (key, value) VALUES (?1, ?2) ON CONFLICT(key) DO UPDATE SET value = excluded.value", params![format!("package:{}", pkg.name), pkg.hash]).map_err(sql)?;
        Ok(())
    };
    let defined = define::define_verified(conn, &pkg.text, &agent, &verify)?;
    Ok(Imported { package: pkg.name, hash: pkg.hash, defined })
}

/// A value of the `meta` table.
pub fn meta(conn: &Connection, key: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, Vec<Diagnostic>> {
    conn.query_row("SELECT value FROM meta WHERE key = ?1", params![key], |r| r.get(0)).optional().map_err(sql)
}
````

insert:

```rust

fn upgrade_2_to_3(tx: &rusqlite::Transaction) -> Result<(), Vec<Diagnostic>> {
    tx.execute_batch(META).map_err(sql)?;
    tx.pragma_update(None, "user_version", 3).map_err(sql)
}

/// Brings a store of an older version to [`SCHEMA_VERSION`] (version 2 -> 3 only creates `meta`).
/// Other versions are left alone (the caller reports them).
pub fn migrate(conn: &mut Connection) -> Result<(), Vec<Diagnostic>> {
    let version: i64 = conn.query_row("PRAGMA user_version", [], |r| r.get(0)).map_err(sql)?;
    if version != 2 {
        return Ok(());
    }
    let tx = conn.transaction_with_behavior(rusqlite::TransactionBehavior::Immediate).map_err(sql)?;
    // Another process may have migrated it meanwhile.
    let version: i64 = tx.query_row("PRAGMA user_version", [], |r| r.get(0)).map_err(sql)?;
    if version == 2 {
        upgrade_2_to_3(&tx)?;
    }
    tx.commit().map_err(sql)
}
```

// Findings of the independent review (Phase 3a), pinned.

#[test]
fn sql_statements_stop_at_the_wall_clock_limit() {
    let src = "fn slow(n: Int) -> Int !db = match db_query(\"WITH RECURSIVE c(x) AS (SELECT 1 UNION ALL SELECT x + 1 FROM c WHERE x < ?) SELECT count(*) FROM c\", [SqlInt(n)]) { Ok(_) => 1, Err(_) => 2 }";
    let r = Runner::prepared(compile(src, Entry::All), Permissions::parse("db:mem").unwrap(), 16 << 20, 2).unwrap().with_timeout(Duration::from_millis(300));
    let (res, took) = on_guest_thread(|| {
        let t = std::time::Instant::now();
        let out = r.execute("slow", &[Value::Int(30_000_000)], ExecIn::default(), &commit_if_ok);
        (out.outcome.result.map_err(|e| e.kind), t.elapsed())
    });
    assert_eq!(res, Err("timeout"));
    assert!(took < Duration::from_millis(1500), "{took:?}");
    // The same database keeps working afterwards (the host's own COMMIT is never interrupted).
    let out = r.invoke("slow", &[Value::Int(10)]);
    assert_eq!(out.result, Ok(Value::Int(1)));
}

#[test]
fn values_sqlite_builds_are_bounded_by_the_memory_limit() {
    let src = "fn big(n: Int) -> Int !db = match db_query(\"SELECT length(randomblob(?))\", [SqlInt(n)]) { Ok(r) => match get(r.rows, 0) { Some(row) => match get(row, 0) { Some(SqlInt(k)) => k, _ => 0 - 2 }, None => 0 - 3 }, Err(DbSql(_)) => 0 - 1, Err(_) => 0 - 4 }";
    let r = Runner::new(compile(src, Entry::All)).unwrap().with_permissions(Permissions::parse("db:mem").unwrap()).with_memory_limit(16 << 20);
    assert_eq!(r.call("big", &[Value::Int(1000)]), Ok(Value::Int(1000)));
    assert_eq!(r.call("big", &[Value::Int(600_000_000)]), Ok(Value::Int(-1)), "string or blob too big");
}

#[test]
fn temp_objects_are_refused() {
    let src = "fn put() -> Int !db = match db_exec(\"CREATE TEMP TABLE IF NOT EXISTS leak AS SELECT 'secret' AS s\", []) { Ok(_) => 1, Err(DbSql(_)) => 0, Err(_) => 2 }";
    let dir = db_project("temp");
    let perms = Permissions::parse("db:t").unwrap().with_db_dir(dir.join(".qal/db"));
    let r = Runner::prepared(compile(src, Entry::All), perms, 16 << 20, 2).unwrap();
    on_guest_thread(|| {
        let db = Db::open(&DbGrant::Named("t".into()), &dir.join(".qal/db"), 16).unwrap();
        let out = r.execute("put", &[], ExecIn { db: Some(db), host_used: 0 }, &commit_if_ok);
        assert_eq!(out.outcome.result, Ok(Value::Int(0)), "TEMP state would outlive the execution on a worker's connection");
    });
}

#[test]
fn decoded_arguments_count_against_the_host_budget() {
    let src = "fn mk(i: Int) -> SqlValue = SqlNull\n\
               fn p(n: Int) -> Int !db = match db_exec(\"SELECT 1\", map(range(0, n), mk)) { Ok(_) => 0, Err(_) => 1 }";
    let r = Runner::new(compile(src, Entry::All)).unwrap().with_permissions(Permissions::parse("db:mem").unwrap()).with_memory_limit(32 << 20);
    assert_eq!(r.call("p", &[Value::Int(10)]), Ok(Value::Int(1)), "10 parameters for none: DbSql");
    let e = r.call("p", &[Value::Int(1_000_000)]).unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!((e.kind, serde_json::to_value(&e.values).unwrap()["host"].clone()), ("out_of_memory", json!(true)));
}
```

insert:

```rust

#[test]
fn kept_alive_clients_do_not_hold_workers_while_others_wait() {
    let dir = project();
    let (p, s, mut srv) = config(&dir);
    srv.workers = 1;
    srv.http.keepalive_idle = Duration::from_millis(800);
    let server = start_with(p, s, srv);
    let addr = server.addr;
    let stop = Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false));
    let attacker = {
        let stop = stop.clone();
        std::thread::spawn(move || {
            let mut a = connect(addr);
            let mut rest = Vec::new();
            while !stop.load(Ordering::SeqCst) {
                if a.write_all(b"GET /health HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: t\r\n\r\n").is_err() || read_resp(&mut a, &mut rest, false).is_none() {
                    return true;
                }
                std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(250));
            }
            false
        })
    };
    std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(300));
    let t = Instant::now();
    let victim = req(addr, "GET", "/health", "");
    let waited = t.elapsed();
    stop.store(true, Ordering::SeqCst);
    assert_eq!(victim.body, "ok");
    // Without the rule the victim would wait until the attacker stops (never, here).
    assert!(waited < Duration::from_secs(2), "the victim waited {waited:?}");
    let _ = attacker.join();
}
```

#[test]
fn packages_never_replace_the_projects_own_definitions() {
    let mut evil = store();
    ok(&mut evil, "fn lib_hello() -> Int = check_password(\"x\")\nfn check_password(p: Str) -> Int = 1");
    let pkg = evil.export("lib_", "lib").unwrap();
    let mut victim = store();
    ok(&mut victim, "fn check_password(p: Str) -> Int = if p == \"s3cret\" then 1 else 0");
    let before = hash(&mut victim, "check_password");
    let d = victim.import(&pkg.file).unwrap_err();
    assert!(d[0].code == "E0243" && d[0].message.contains("would replace the project's own `check_password`"), "{d:?}");
    assert_eq!(hash(&mut victim, "check_password"), before);
    // A newer version of the same package may replace what the older one defined.
    let mut lib = store();
    ok(&mut lib, "fn lib_one() -> Int = 1");
    let v1 = lib.export("lib_", "lib").unwrap();
    ok(&mut lib, "fn lib_one() -> Int = 2");
    let v2 = lib.export("lib_", "lib").unwrap();
    let mut user = store();
    user.import(&v1.file).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(statuses(&user.import(&v2.file).unwrap().defined), vec![("lib_one", "replaced")]);
}
```

        };

        let deadline: Arc<Mutex<Option<Instant>>> = Arc::new(Mutex::new(None));
        let watched = deadline.clone();
        conn.progress_handler(
            PROGRESS_STEPS,
            Some(move || watched.lock().map(|d| d.is_some_and(|d| Instant::now() >= d)).unwrap_or(true)),

        Ok(Db { conn, in_tx: false, deadline })
    }

    /// Sets the limits of the next execution: statements are interrupted at `deadline`, and no
    /// string or blob SQLite builds may be larger than `max_value` bytes (SQLite allocates those
    /// outside the host's accounting).
    pub fn limit(&self, deadline: Option<Instant>, max_value: usize) {
        if let Ok(mut d) = self.deadline.lock() {
            *d = deadline;
        }
        let _ = self.conn.set_limit(rusqlite::limits::Limit::SQLITE_LIMIT_LENGTH, max_value.clamp(1024, 1 << 30) as i32);
    }

fn read_value(c: &mut Caller<'_, HostState>, ty: &Type, ptr: i32) -> Result<Value, wasmtime::Error> {
    let mem = memory(c)?;
    let max = c.data().limit;

```rust
}

/// Reads a value of a prelude-based type from guest memory, exactly; the host copy counts against
/// the host budget.
fn read_value(c: &mut Caller<'_, HostState>, ty: &Type, ptr: i32) -> Result<Value, wasmtime::Error> {
    let mem = memory(c)?;
    let room = c.data().limit.saturating_sub(c.data().host_used);
    match decode_exact_counted(prelude_program(), mem.data(&c), ty, ptr as u32 as i64, room) {
        Ok((v, bytes)) => {
            charge(c, bytes)?;
            Ok(v)
        }
        Err(e) if e.starts_with("the value is larger") => {
            let requested = c.data().host_used.saturating_add(room).saturating_add(1);
            Err(host_oom(c, requested))
        }
        Err(e) => Err(wasmtime::Error::msg(format!("cannot read the argument: {e}"))),
    }
}

        [0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, a, b] if (a, b) != (0, 0) && (a, b) != (0, 1) => Some(v4(a, b)),
```

In `qal/crates/qal-run/src/value.rs`, replace:

```rust
}

/// Decodes a value the host acts on (an `HttpCall`, SQL parameters, a handler's `HttpResponse`):
/// exactly, without cutting strings, and failing on anything malformed or on more than `max_bytes`
/// (string bytes plus 8 per node).
pub fn decode_exact(tp: &TProgram, mem: &[u8], ty: &Type, slot: i64, max_bytes: usize) -> Result<Value, String> {
    let mut used = 0usize;
    exact_at(tp, mem, ty, slot, 0, max_bytes, &mut used)
}

fn exact_at(tp: &TProgram, mem: &[u8], ty: &Type, slot: i64, depth: usize, max: usize, used: &mut usize) -> Result<Value, String> {
    *used = used.saturating_add(8);
    if *used > max {
        return Err(format!("the value is larger than {max} bytes"));
```

with:

```rust
}

/// Host bytes one decoded node is counted as (the `Value` plus its share of names and vectors).
pub const NODE_BYTES: usize = 64;

/// Decodes a value the host acts on (an `HttpCall`, SQL parameters, a handler's `HttpResponse`):
/// exactly, without cutting strings, and failing on anything malformed or on more than `max_bytes`
/// (string bytes plus [`NODE_BYTES`] per node).
pub fn decode_exact(tp: &TProgram, mem: &[u8], ty: &Type, slot: i64, max_bytes: usize) -> Result<Value, String> {
    decode_exact_counted(tp, mem, ty, slot, max_bytes).map(|(v, _)| v)
}

/// [`decode_exact`], also returning the host bytes the value is counted as.
pub fn decode_exact_counted(tp: &TProgram, mem: &[u8], ty: &Type, slot: i64, max_bytes: usize) -> Result<(Value, usize), String> {
    let mut used = 0usize;
    let v = exact_at(tp, mem, ty, slot, 0, max_bytes, &mut used)?;
    Ok((v, used))
}

fn exact_at(tp: &TProgram, mem: &[u8], ty: &Type, slot: i64, depth: usize, max: usize, used: &mut usize) -> Result<Value, String> {
    *used = used.saturating_add(NODE_BYTES);
    if *used > max {
        return Err(format!("the value is larger than {max} bytes"));
```

---

## Done When

- `cd qal && cargo test --workspace`: every one of the 21 test binaries `ok`, 318 tests in total; `cargo build --workspace --all-targets` without warnings; the no-panic clippy command clean.
- The performance gate holds in a release build (measured 10.783µs ≤ 200 µs).
- `qal serve` answers HTTP from a stored handler with one fresh instance per request, the documented limits, rejections and log lines, and stops gracefully on SIGTERM; `qal request` and `qal_request` run the same pipeline without TCP.
- `net` and `db` work only with their grants; SQL is literal-only; SSRF, smuggling, slowloris, isolation and budget tests pass.
- `qal export` / `qal import` round-trip packages with identical hashes; `qal/std/std.qalpkg` is the empty valid package.
- Phase 3b can start: it adds the `std` namespace, E0240 for `std.` names, `meta.role`, auto-import and E0244 on top of this.
